# Software (CPU) Rasterizer Graphics Backend — Implementation Plan

> **Current status (Software <-> EasyGL XNA/Core parity campaign, 2026-09-07..12): HIGH PARITY; SPECIFIC CLASSIC-XNA GAPS REMAIN.**
> The original `SOFTWARE-1..86` rows below remain the historical record of the deliberately small
> v1 renderer. They are not the current completion boundary. The authorized target is now an
> independent, deterministic CPU implementation of the classic XNA 4.0/core graphics behavior on
> which an application can rely wherever it can rely on EasyGL, except for explicitly classified
> non-applicable window/GPU behavior and deferred CNAEXT-modern facilities.

> storage, viewport/scissor, wireframe, depth bias, 16/32-bit indexed addressing, dynamic buffer

## Software <-> EasyGL XNA/Core parity campaign

### Scope and evidence rules

This section is the current executable plan. It supersedes the old v1 authorization boundary while
preserving `SOFTWARE-1..86` below as history. Classic XNA 4.0 behavior and renderer-agnostic CNA
core plumbing are authorized. `CNA::Graphics`, `modules/graphics-ext`, arbitrary `ShaderEffect`
execution, PBR effects, compute/storage buffers, HDR/post-processing, modern shadows/IBL and other
CNAEXT engine features are explicitly deferred and must not be used to inflate the parity target.

Evidence authority is Microsoft XNA 4.0 measurements/IL, then FNA, then EasyGL, then existing
Software behavior, then prose. A green row requires behavioral proof; the existence of a method or
a test that merely records an unsupported boundary is not parity. The durable family-by-family
classification and evidence is in `docs/software-easygl-parity-ledger.md`.

### Baseline record

- Branch: `software`.
- Start commit: `cde325ecd4d771d50b7d8ec3ef99f1657ad217ac`.
- Starting tracked worktree: clean.
- Build dependency note: CNA at this commit requires sharp-runtime's `Xml.Serialization`
  component, which is absent from the sibling `develop` checkout (`df1b42ab`) but present in the
  existing sibling `next` worktree (`7e9c58fd`). Baseline builds therefore use
  `-DCNA_SHARP_RUNTIME_ROOT=/rv/data/development/github.com/openeggbert/sharp-runtimenext`.
- The first configure established that the pinned CNA submodules were absent; they were initialized
  at the revisions recorded by this repository without changing a tracked CNA file. All campaign
  builds used `/rv/cnaccache` through ccache and at most two compile jobs.
- The final Software configuration builds. Its complete `Software` CTest label passes 156/156 with
  `-j 2` under an authenticated TCP Xvfb, and `CnaGraphicsTests` passes 2,324 with 54 intentional
  renderer-specific skips out of 2,378. The renderer-native 64×64 differential dump differs from
  Mesa EasyGL by at most one byte per channel and passes the tightened default comparator.
- The OPENGLES3/EasyGL comparison configuration at `cmake-build-easygl` builds against the same
  sharp-runtime and ccache inputs. The last clean monolithic checkpoint passed 2,336 tests with 58
  intentional skips out of 2,394. The final authenticated-Xvfb run after `SOFTWARE-157` repeats
  that result exactly with zero failures. The formerly order-sensitive video-reference fixture
  also passes five consecutive in-process runs (30 passes and five legitimate single-renderer
  skips). No pixel, API, shared-code, lifecycle or parity assertion failed.
- GDI cannot be configured natively on this Linux host. The supported MinGW configuration succeeds,
  but both the focused stencil target and the renderer-only target are currently blocked before any
  GDI/CNA source is compiled by sibling sharp-runtime commit `7e9c58fd` unconditionally including
  POSIX `poll.h` from `System/Diagnostics/Process.cpp`. Software's shared 2D state source is still
  covered by the native Software build; this external GDI cross-build blocker is recorded rather
  than misreported as a stencil regression.
- The final broad Software `CnaTests` run under authenticated Xvfb executed 8,816 tests after
  excluding one separately measured pathological CNAEXT GamerServices case: 8,739 passed, 72 were
  intentionally skipped and five failed. Three failures inspect deferred modern code rather than
  rendering it (`GltfRendererPbrFallbackPolicy.EveryPbrRendererHonorsCallerOwnedCullState` and two
  obsolete Software-invalid expectations for custom `ShaderEffect` content); the other two are the
  unrelated ContentManager `StringReader` registration and `PacketReader` Color decoding tests.
  The excluded `GuideTest.TheClickThatAnswersAMessageBoxDoesNotAlsoReachTheGame` scans the complete
  viewport in four-pixel steps and renders the overlay twice at every point; it made no progress
  past its test body within an isolated 30-second limit and made the unfiltered CPU run impractical.
  No broad-suite failure exercises Software rasterization or a claimed XNA/Core capability.

### Final adversarial challenge baseline

- Challenge start commit: `92ed418b3dfcd4a2e03ea8c0d092a0b4caca9e9f`, branch `software`,
  clean tracked and untracked worktree.
- The plan contained 94 explicit `SOFTWARE-*` rows at challenge start: 91 complete, one blocked
  (`SOFTWARE-100`) and two intentionally pending historical options (`SOFTWARE-85/86`). The
  highest assigned identifier was `SOFTWARE-157`.
- The existing Software configuration built. Without a display, 155 of 156 `Software`-label
  CTests passed; `Software_PresentLifecycle` alone was not executable because its expected-abort
  child also exited with GoogleTest's skip code after SDL could not create an X11 window. The
  preceding campaign's authenticated-Xvfb result remains 156/156 and will be repeated before the
  challenge verdict.
- The existing OPENGLES3 EasyGL configuration built with compiled effects disabled. A separate
  desktop `OPENGL33` configuration was created to exercise desktop-only state and a dependency-
  enabled `OPENGL33` configuration was created with `CNA_EASYGL_COMPILED_EFFECTS=ON` for the
  classic-Effect audit. This matters because the option defaults off only to avoid pulling
  MojoShader into an otherwise dependency-free renderer; it is not a claim that EasyGL lacks the
  feature.

### Executable backlog

The order is dependency-led: truthful reporting, primitive/input foundations, raster correctness,
classic effects/state, resources, then high-level orchestration. Each implementation row includes
its own tests and plan evidence in the same commit.

| SOFTWARE-110 | Make 4x MSAA depth/stencil and coverage sample-correct | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-07. Software now allocates four independent color, float-depth and 8-bit-stencil samples per pixel, clears every sample, evaluates triangle coverage and plane depth at the same rotated 2x2 locations, applies `MultiSampleMask` before depth/stencil, and executes the full stencil tuple independently on each active sample. Resolve stays deterministic and switching back to one sample preserves sample zero while releasing all optional planes. The renderer-neutral `MsaaFragmentContract` passes 8/8 on Software and Mesa EasyGL: each individual mask bit resolves to one quarter, a near write to sample 0 leaves farther samples 1..3 writable, a geometric edge retains independent depth, and one stamped stencil sample leaves the other three writable. The storage fixture passes 5/5 and the focused Software set passes 12/12 CTests. The same audit found that EasyGL silently ignored `MultiSampleMask`; it now uses `glSampleMaski` when available and rejects non-default masks on incapable profiles. | SOFTWARE-111 | Implement complete `AlphaTestEffect` comparison semantics | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-07 and corrected by SOFTWARE-303. Software evaluates FNA's encoded four-value alpha-test expression after texture × vertex × effect alpha and before every depth/stencil operation, colour write, blend or fog-stage work. The renderer-neutral `AlphaTestEffectContract` passes 30/30 on both Software and Mesa EasyGL: all eight `CompareFunction` values at below/equal/above byte-reference alpha, half-byte equality tolerance, texture/effect/vertex composition, disabled vertex colour, Microsoft XNA's opaque-black null texture with alpha one, and discarded-near-fragment depth preservation. Full observable stencil-state coverage remains correctly owned by `SOFTWARE-121`; the shared fragment ordering already places discard before that state path. |
| SOFTWARE-112 | Implement classic stock-effect fog | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-07, with alpha-fog oracle follow-up on 2026-09-08. Software now computes FNA's saturated fog dot product once per vertex from the post-skin object-space position, carries the keep factor through homogeneous clipping and perspective-correct interpolation, then mixes `FogColor * outputAlpha` into the final stock-effect RGB after texture/material/environment-map and alpha-test processing but before blending. `BasicEffect`, `AlphaTestEffect`, `DualTextureEffect`, `EnvironmentMapEffect` and `SkinnedEffect` share the renderer-neutral `StockEffectFogContract`: 33/33 on Software and 33/33 on Mesa EasyGL, covering disabled fog, start/end boundaries, identity and transformed `World`/`View` midpoints, `FogStart == FogEnd`, SkinnedEffect's post-bone position, final-stage environment-map ordering and transparent premultiplied fog colour. The last probe exposed an EasyGL bug against FNA `Common.fxh`; all ten classic EasyGL stock shader variants now multiply fog colour by the completed alpha too, while CNAEXT PBR is unchanged. Software differs from Mesa by at most one byte from RGBA8 truncation versus rounding; the asserted tolerance is two, not a broad image tolerance. Nine affected Software CTests pass, as do the six pre-existing EasyGL fog binaries after the shared fix. The GDI 2D subset receives only the default no-op factor; its cross-build remains blocked in sharp-runtime before CNA compilation as recorded above. |
| SOFTWARE-113 | Implement full `BasicEffect` lighting | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. Software now evaluates FNA `Lighting.fxh` on the CPU: all three enabled-gated directional diffuse/specular inputs, ambient × material diffuse, emissive, material specular/power and eye-dependent half vectors. `World` normals use the same inverse-transpose cofactor transform as EasyGL. The XNA-default path computes and saturates diffuse/specular at each vertex before clipping and perspective interpolation; `PreferPerPixelLighting=true` instead interpolates normalized world position/normal and evaluates the equation per fragment. Texture, vertex colour and alpha follow the stock shader ordering, specular is added with completed alpha, and fog follows the complete lit colour. The renderer-neutral `BasicEffectLightingContract` passes 15/15 on both Software and Mesa EasyGL with a two-byte RGBA8 tolerance: unlit emissive behavior, ambient/emissive, all three lights, negative `NdotL`, non-uniform scale, texture+vertex colour+alpha, two specular powers, per-vertex/per-pixel dispatch, lit fog and both faces of default lighting. Ten focused Software CTests and the `CnaGraphicsTests` BasicEffect filter (35/35) pass; the full Software label is 70 pass/four existing skips/only the known display-dependent PresentLifecycle failure. Seven pre-existing EasyGL BasicEffect binaries pass. GDI cross-build remains externally blocked before CNA by sharp-runtime's unconditional POSIX `poll.h`. |
| SOFTWARE-114 | Complete `EnvironmentMapEffect` classic lighting and normal semantics | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. Software now matches FNA's vertex shader rather than reconstructing an approximation per fragment: it transforms normals by inverse-transpose `World`, evaluates ambient/emissive/material colour plus all three enabled directional diffuse lights, computes the eye-relative reflection vector and Fresnel scalar per vertex, clips those varyings in homogeneous space, and perspective-interpolates them into the cube-map pixel equation. The D3D9 `COLOR0/COLOR1` saturation boundary is preserved, including amounts below zero or above one. Texture/effect alpha scales both the cube lerp target and environment-specular term, with the already-complete fog stage last. The renderer-neutral `EnvironmentMapEffectContract` passes 14/14 on both Software and Mesa EasyGL with a two-byte RGBA8 tolerance: material ordering, light enable/direction/three-light sum, negative `NdotL`, amount saturation, non-uniform-scale lighting/reflection, alpha/specular composition and three per-vertex Fresnel probes. | SOFTWARE-157 | Bind the shared `GraphicsAdapter` video pin to ambient-platform lifecycle | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. The final EasyGL audit's fresh-process sharding exposed that `GraphicsAdapter` represented its long-lived video reference with one process-global boolean and released it through whichever platform happened to be current. After a `ScopedCurrentPlatform`/Game transition that could release the replacement's reference, strand the predecessor's reference, and leave cached display IDs without the session that issued them. `CurrentPlatform` now owns renderer-agnostic, token-keyed subsystem pins: a platform transition acquires the replacement before releasing the predecessor, repeated pinning is idempotent, a failed transfer is retried on the next real-adapter access, and reset/unpin releases exactly the recorded owner. Deterministic platform tests prove acquisition order, idempotence and reset cleanup. Graphics-device lifecycle tests separately measure the persistent adapter pin and each device-owned reference, including lazy retry. The complete Software graphics suite remains 2,324 pass/54 intentional skips and the Software CTest label remains 156/156; the EasyGL lifecycle fixture passes five consecutive runs (30 executed tests plus five legitimate single-renderer skips) under Mesa/Xvfb. |

| SOFTWARE-161 | Assess classic compiled XNA `Effect` parity independently of CNAEXT `ShaderEffect` | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08; the broad parity claim is disproved. With `CNA_EASYGL_COMPILED_EFFECTS=ON`, EasyGL uses pinned MojoShader's effect parser and OpenGL adapter for reflection, parameters, techniques, passes, state assignments and translated SM1-3 vertex/pixel programs. Its complete 37-test suite passes on Mesa desktop GL, including exact pixels, ordinary/indexed/multi-stream/instanced draws, SpriteBatch custom effects, render-target/cube/volume samplers and context recreation. `EffectReader`, `EffectMaterialReader` and model shared resources expose the path to content and model materials. Genuine parity therefore requires a reusable D3D9 shader-token interpreter integrated with Software's vertex, clipping, quad-fragment, sampler and MRT stages. | SOFTWARE-162 | Build a Software compiled-effect runtime and reusable D3D9 shader-token IR | ✅ | **Completed 2026-09-11 without making a false capability claim.** `CNA_SOFTWARE_COMPILED_EFFECTS=ON` now opts the CPU renderer into the same pinned MojoShader Effect Framework parser and renderer-neutral reflection/state translator used by the established compiled backends. A headless shader backend retains each original little-endian D3D9 token stream, validates its stage/model and complete instruction/comment/END boundaries, and publishes the selected vertex/pixel program plus the float/int/bool register files needed by the next interpreter phases. The runtime implements parameters, sampler/render-state assignments, techniques/passes, preshader evaluation, independent cloning and deterministic disposal through `ICompiledEffectRuntime`. Its parser profile is GLSL 1.20 only because this pinned build disables MojoShader's D3D/bytecode emitters; generated GLSL is discarded and never executed or treated as the Software IR. | SOFTWARE-167 | Audit the public vertex-stage texture and sampler collections | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08; a renderer-wide classic-XNA gap was confirmed. The Software runtime and both focused EasyGL tests pass; the complete EasyGL compiled-effect family passes 60/60, full Software graphics passes 2,625/2,688 with 63 classified skips, and full desktop EasyGL graphics passes 2,635/2,688 with 53 renderer/platform skips. | SOFTWARE-169 | Match XNA/FNA's opaque-factor blend-enable rule | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. The adversarial property-combination pass found that Software's fast path required One/Zero **and Add** before bypassing blending, while both FNA3D's OpenGL and D3D11 drivers—and EasyGL—derive BlendEnable from the four factors alone. Thus a One/Zero `BlendState` with `ReverseSubtract` or `Min` incorrectly produced zero RGB/alpha on Software instead of the unblended source. The shared matrix now varies all five functions under opaque factors and checks both RGB and alpha: it exposed four pre-fix Software failures and now passes 45/45 on Software and Mesa desktop EasyGL. Software's reference-compatible bypass now deliberately ignores the function whenever both channels use One/Zero. |
| SOFTWARE-170 | Ignore blend factors for `BlendFunction.Min` and `Max` | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. Software multiplied source and destination by their selected factors before every operation, but D3D9/11 and OpenGL MIN/MAX compare the raw components and ignore both factors; FNA3D maps XNA's functions directly to those operations. New zero-factor cases made the distinction deterministic and failed 4/49 before repair (Min/Max independently for RGB and alpha). The CPU blend stage now bypasses factor evaluation for those two functions. The expanded shared matrix passes 49/49 on Software and Mesa desktop EasyGL, while the other 35 factor/function cases and opaque-factor quirk remain covered in the same draw path. |
| SOFTWARE-171 | Accept XNA's signed indexed `baseVertex` contract | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. The recovered Microsoft XNA 4.0 `GraphicsDevice` passes `baseVertex` unchanged to Direct3D 9 as a signed value and documents it as the offset added to each index; FNA does the same. CNA instead rejected every negative value in both indexed entry points and Software repeated the rejection, even when `baseVertex + minVertexIndex` and every decoded address were valid. Both public paths now validate signed effective starts in 64-bit arithmetic; Software separately validates every decoded index plus base before reading CPU memory. The ordinary regression uses indices 3..5 with base -3 to select a green triangle instead of a same-position red decoy, and the instanced regression uses indices 12..20 with base -12 to select slots 0..2 instead of 4..6. Both pass on Software and Mesa desktop EasyGL; the surrounding range suites pass 22/24 on Software (two intentional foreign-renderer skips) and 35/40 on EasyGL (five existing renderer-specific skips), while underflowing ranges and decoded addresses remain rejected. |
| SOFTWARE-172 | Reject non-positive texture and render-target dimensions before native allocation | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. Recovered Microsoft XNA 4.0 checks every `Texture2D` width/height, `TextureCube` size and `Texture3D` width/height/depth for values less than one and throws `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` before profile/native allocation. CNA instead delegated the question to each renderer: Software produced renderer-specific standard exceptions and EasyGL could issue invalid zero-sized GL allocation calls. The shared constructors now perform the XNA check first. `RenderTarget2D` and `RenderTargetCube` repeat it in their pre-base allocation helpers because C++ evaluates those native-factory arguments before the `Texture2D`/`TextureCube` base constructor can run. Five focused tests cover zero and negative values across both overloads and all five resource kinds; they pass 5/5 on Software and OPENGLES3 EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-173 | Implement all XNA HiDef `Texture3D` storage and typed transfer formats | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-10. The pre-fix shared constructor contract produced nineteen failures on Software: all fourteen non-Color XNA HiDef volume formats threw the Color-only generic runtime error, while the five forbidden DXT/normalized formats also exposed the wrong exception boundary. Recovered XNA/FNA's exact fifteen-format `ValidVolumeFormats` table now has a resource-specific profile gate and renderer verdict. `Texture3D` exposes packed-vector and float/Vector2/Vector4 full/window/box/mip transfers, serializes CNA's object-shaped XNA values into their exact native words, and applies XNA's total-byte rule: the element width may divide the format width when the element count supplies exactly the box's bytes. | SOFTWARE-174 | Make EasyGL's GLES/WebGL signed-base fallback handle negative bases | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. `SOFTWARE-171` made the public signed contract reachable and proved Software plus desktop EasyGL, but the first GLES execution rendered garbage for both ordinary and instanced draws: EasyGL tried to emulate every base with an attribute-pointer shift, which cannot represent a compensated negative base without an address before the vertex buffer. EasyGL now copies only the selected CPU-shadowed index slice, adds the negative base with checked 64-bit arithmetic, uploads it to a context-loss-safe scratch element buffer and draws it from offset zero. Positive fallback bases retain the allocation-free pointer shift; desktop and compiled-effect routes share the same safe negative-index path. OPENGLES3 passes ordinary 16-bit, ordinary 32-bit with nonzero `startIndex`, and instanced regressions; Software passes the same three. Desktop passes each corrected draw, while stress sequencing exposed a separate pre-existing raw-GL entry-point lifecycle defect filed as SOFTWARE-175. |
| SOFTWARE-175 | Stop caching desktop point-size GL dispatch across EasyGL contexts | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. Repeating any public 32-bit indexed EasyGL draw destroyed its device successfully but the next desktop device jumped through address zero in `EnableVertexProgramPointSize()`. A normal-speed signal trace resolved the null call's saved return address to the raw `glEnable` pointer cached by that helper; debugger timing masked the failure. The helper now resolves that entry point from the current context on every renderer construction, so no address survives its owning GL context. A single regression performs a real Uint32 draw, destroys every resource and its device, then constructs and clears a replacement device in the same process. It fails by SIGSEGV before the repair and passes afterward; the prior provoking Uint32 test repeats three times, as do the 16/32-bit signed-base pair and ordinary/instanced set. |
| SOFTWARE-176 | Apply `RasterizerState.DepthBias` in XNA's normalized depth units | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-08. The prior Software implementation multiplied the public value by `2^-24`, while EasyGL passed it unscaled to `glPolygonOffset`'s native-unit argument. Both interpretations contradict FNA3D: its GL and D3D11 drivers multiply the public float by `(2^depthBits)-1`, proving XNA exposes normalized post-viewport depth rather than one minimum-resolvable unit. The original Software regression used `3000000.0f`, accidentally compensating for the defect. Software now adds the public constant directly to normalized CPU depth. EasyGL retains it, scales it by 65,535 or 16,777,215 for the active Depth16/Depth24 destination, and reapplies on 2D, cube, MRT and backbuffer transitions. A shared target-switch oracle uses realistic ±0.02 values and proves nearer/biased-behind and farther/biased-ahead results on all three depth formats; it passes on Software, Mesa OpenGL33 and Mesa OpenGLES3. The compiled sprite draw also passes the live collection after each effect pass. CNA instead mutated all four public device states during every `Begin()`. The new all-state discriminator failed all four pre-End assertions before repair. SpriteBatch retains the resolved state payloads, applies them during Immediate Begin or before every non-Immediate End flush, and configures its private renderer from the same sampler payload. The test proves the prior blend/sampler/depth/rasterizer values survive Deferred Begin and all four requested values become public at empty End; SOFTWARE-187's two Immediate sampler transitions remain green. SOFTWARE-232 subsequently made first application freeze the shared payload, as Microsoft XNA requires. |

The two-device tests pass on Software and desktop EasyGL. Full `CnaGraphicsTests` passes 2,430 with 55 classified skips out of 2,485 on Software and 2,442 with 59 skips out of 2,501 on desktop EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-224 | Restore Microsoft XNA `SetVertexBuffers` validation and failure-state semantics | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-09. CNA's FNA-led contract explicitly accepted null entries as unused slots, used `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` above 16 streams, prevalidated the entire array transactionally, and rejected `SetVertexBuffer(nullptr, negativeOffset)` before unbinding. Recovered Microsoft XNA instead throws `ArgumentException` at the first null slot, `NotSupportedException` above the profile's 16-stream ceiling, applies entries in order and retains only the processed prefix on failure, and ignores the offset when the singular buffer is null. Three pre-fix unit discriminators failed across all four distinctions. Full `CnaGraphicsTests` remains 2,430/2,485 with 55 classified skips on Software and 2,442/2,501 with 59 skips on desktop EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-225 | Restore XNA texture/sampler collection profile, format and lifecycle boundaries | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-09. XNA exposes 16 pixel samplers but constructs zero vertex texture/sampler slots under Reach and four under HiDef; vertex texture fetch additionally permits only seven float/half formats. CNA created 16 slots for both stages in every profile, accepted Color vertex textures, checked index bounds before the supplied texture's disposal, and let an owned texture collection remain readable/writable after its GraphicsDevice was disposed. This task closed collection bookkeeping; SOFTWARE-168 subsequently closed and behaviorally proved execution. |
| SOFTWARE-226 | Restore Microsoft XNA viewport/scissor validation and lifetime semantics | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-09. Recovered Microsoft XNA validates every assigned viewport against the active backbuffer or first render target, requires positive dimensions, an ordered 0..1 depth range, and validates scissor coordinates/extents against that same surface while allowing a zero-area boundary rectangle. Shared overflow-safe validation now runs before renderer mutation and commits only accepted state, target dimensions take precedence over the larger backbuffer, and both getters enforce device lifetime. The shared getters now apply the same `ObjectDisposedException` boundary. The expanded renderer-facing lifecycle contract passes on Software and desktop EasyGL, and the unchanged full-suite baselines remain 2,436/2,491 with 55 classified skips and 2,448/2,507 with 59 skips respectively. The deliberately cached `Adapter`, `GraphicsProfile`, `PresentationParameters`, state, mask/factor and buffer properties remain readable where the recovered Microsoft getters do not perform this native-resource check. |
| SOFTWARE-228 | Unbind active render targets during `GraphicsDevice.Reset` | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-09. Recovered Microsoft XNA saves ordinary draw state, calls `SetRenderTargets(null, 0)` before resetting the backbuffer, and deliberately omits render targets from `SavedDeviceState.Restore`. CNA left `currentRenderTargets_` and `renderTargetBound_` intact while resizing/reconfiguring the renderer. A reset performed with an 8x6 target bound therefore continued to report that target and made the next `Present()` throw, even though the renderer had established a backbuffer. The public lifecycle discriminator passes on Software and desktop EasyGL; full suites pass 2,437/2,492 with 55 classified skips and 2,449/2,508 with 59 skips respectively. |

Reading the complete decompiled Microsoft XNA `Apply` bodies falsified that interpretation: BlendState, DepthStencilState, RasterizerState and SamplerState all replace `_parent` with the new device, set `isBound`, and rebuild device-derived native fields; no exception is raised. | SOFTWARE-244 | Restore XNA `GetBackBufferData` validation and active-target semantics | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-09. | SOFTWARE-249 | Restore the classic `VertexBuffer` generic/window transfer surface and stride semantics | ✅ | Completed 2026-09-09. Every recovered Microsoft Boolean/Int32/Single, Vector2/3/4, Quaternion and Matrix array getter allocates exactly the caller's positive `count`, copies at most the reflected parameter's available values and leaves the remainder zero-filled. Vector-shaped results are packed from one flat logical source stream, so a single `float4` read as Vector2 values yields `(x,y)`, `(z,w)`, then zeros rather than one result followed by truncation. CNA instead appended only complete values while storage remained and returned a shorter vector; scalar arrays also read raw target bits instead of applying the destination getter's conversion. Exact XNA exception identities, MRT limits, declared surface formats and cube orientation are asserted without broad catches. The configured `DISPLAY=:90` corpus passes 333/336; the three remaining failures (`SamplerComponentIsolation`, `AnisotropicFilterContract`, negative `MaxMipLevel`) all pass when their unchanged binaries run on a fresh Xvfb/Mesa context, proving a display/driver split rather than 333 hidden regressions. The negative-mip driver dependency is nevertheless made deterministic by follow-up `SOFTWARE-285`; the other two are extension-defined anisotropic footprint differences and retain strict behavioral coverage on the clean context. |
| SOFTWARE-285 | Make EasyGL negative `SamplerState.MaxMipLevel` deterministic across GL drivers | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** `SamplerState.MaxMipLevel=-1` is written through XNA/D3D9's unsigned state channel and must clamp sampling to the resource's least-detailed stored level. EasyGL converted `uint32_t(-1)` directly to `float` for `GL_TEXTURE_MIN_LOD`; the resulting roughly 4.29-billion value selected the wrong level on the configured Mesa display even though a fresh Xvfb context happened to clamp it correctly. The unsigned request is now capped at GL's standard default upper LOD of 1000 before float conversion. That remains far above XNA's largest possible mip index while avoiding out-of-range driver behavior. The shared 93-check mip contract passes on both displays, including stock 3D and SpriteBatch negative-to-last-level assertions. |
| SOFTWARE-286 | Restore compiled-Effect fuzz/benchmark tools after pointer-valued XNA collection indexers | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The broad EasyGL build exposed two non-test targets that had not followed `SOFTWARE-255`'s XNA-faithful pointer-valued `EffectParameterCollection`, `EffectTechniqueCollection`, `EffectPassCollection` and annotation indexers. `compiled_effect_fuzzer` and `compiled_effect_benchmark` still used reference/member syntax and failed compilation. Both tools now dereference the public pointer contract explicitly; the fuzzer also guards every reflected collection element because malformed native parser input is its purpose, while the benchmark validates its required first pass once. The normal and compiled-effect desktop configurations build both targets. The real compiled-effect configuration replays all 26 stock/conformance/crash-corpus binaries and the benchmark completes construction, clone, dirty/clean pass application and compiled/stock draw measurements. A full 571-step EasyGL rebuild succeeds. |
| SOFTWARE-287 | Detach destroyed C++ texture and buffer wrappers from public device bindings | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The lifecycle corpus covered explicit `Dispose()` but manually unbound the disposed vertex/index wrappers before their C++ destructors. It therefore missed a renderer-neutral dangling-state defect: `GraphicsResource::~GraphicsResource` executes after derived destruction and dispatches only the base cleanup, so `Texture2D`/`TextureCube`/`Texture3D` destructor-only paths never ran `Texture::Dispose`, and vertex/index destructors never cleared the device's raw binding pointers. FNA deliberately retains an explicitly disposed buffer object in its managed binding, but that managed object remains strongly reachable; CNA must preserve that observable state only while its pointer-valued wrapper still exists. The three texture destructors now run their complete non-throwing cleanup, buffer destructors detach single/multi-stream or index state before releasing their renderer, and a weak device-lifetime token prevents a resource that outlives its device from calling through a dead owner while retaining XNA's public `GraphicsDevice` identity after disposal. The shared lifecycle test proves pixel- and vertex-stage texture detachment, FNA's explicit-dispose/live-wrapper buffer state, post-destruction clearing and whole-set invalidation when any vertex stream dies. It passes 24/24 on Software and EasyGL; handle release (31/31), move smoke (21/21) and device-first disposal (11/11) remain green on both. Full graphics pass 2,523/2,587 on Software and 2,534/2,603 on EasyGL GLES, with only the same 64/69 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-288 | Transfer GraphicsDevice ownership and live bindings when C++ graphics resources move | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The old move smoke test observed only renderer-handle ownership while source and destination lived in one scope. A scope-crossing probe failed 9 of its 12 new lifecycle assertions: `GraphicsDevice::resources_`, vertex/index bindings and sampler slots retained the moved-from wrapper address, so destroying that source silently untracked and unbound the still-live destination; later device disposal could no longer reach it. `GraphicsResource` now transfers or coalesces its tracking entry and leaves the source inert. `VertexBuffer`, `IndexBuffer`, `Texture2D`, `Texture3D` and `TextureCube` transfer every applicable raw binding while preserving vertex offset/frequency; `RenderTargetBinding` follows moved 2D targets without rebinding or replacing their native storage. The expanded shared test covers construction and assignment, same-scope handle ownership, source-lifetime boundaries, exact one-entry tracking, single/multi-stream buffers, pixel/vertex samplers, 2D/volume/cube textures and an actively bound `RenderTarget2D`; it passes 60/60 on Software and EasyGL GLES. Two shared unit discriminators additionally prove cross-device move-assignment detaches the overwritten wrapper from its former device and that a bound destination render target is rejected before mutation rather than leaving a native/public binding mismatch. Bound-resource disposal, handle release, device-first disposal and leak tests pass 5/5 on each renderer. Full graphics pass 2,525/2,589 on Software and 2,536/2,605 on EasyGL GLES, with the same 64/69 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-289 | Reconcile the exact Software graphics skip ledger with the current compiled-Effect corpus | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The final-challenge ledger still described the earlier 55-skip baseline even though nine later compiled-Effect conformance tests had deliberately expanded the current Software result to 2,525 pass/64 skips. Exactly 49 skips remain platform/build-shape restrictions, foreign-renderer selectors, negative capability controls or CNAEXT paths; the other 15 are the six `EffectMaterialTest` and nine `EffectTest` compiled-bytecode cases, all gated solely by truthful `GraphicsCapability::CompiledEffects == false` and all mapped to the existing classic backlog SOFTWARE-162..165. The ledger now names both compiled families and every member, preserves the exact 49/15 split, and no longer understates the acknowledged classic compiled-Effect gap. No new stock-effect, state, resource or capability gap is hidden by a skip. |
| SOFTWARE-290 | Restore Microsoft vertex/index buffer `CopyData<T>` validation and precedence | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** SOFTWARE-204 correctly repaired zero-capacity construction but retained FNA's empty-transfer no-op and rewrote two substantial test suites to require it. Recovered Microsoft `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer.CopyData<T>` instead checks disposed state, rejects a null or empty managed array, rejects a bound non-streaming write, rejects WriteOnly readback, and then calls `Helpers.ValidateCopyParameters`, which reports `dataIndex` before `elementCount` and requires `elementCount > 0`. CNA accepted null/zero as a no-op, exposed `startIndex` as the parameter name, and let range or WriteOnly validation beat the recovered higher-priority failures. Two new mixed-invalid contract tests failed 0/2 before repair (18 individual exception checks). Shared classic typed/generic vertex and index routes now enforce disposed → null → bound/write-only → `dataIndex` → positive `elementCount` before stride/capacity work; raw CNAEXT vertex transfers retain their explicit zero-count behavior. The two old empty-data suites now assert Microsoft rather than FNA semantics and rejected calls preserve the last real upload. The complete focused selection passes 53/53 on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL; all three configurations build cleanly. Full Software graphics passes 2,527/2,591 with the same 64 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-291 | Restore Microsoft buffer byte-offset validation order and failure identity | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The SOFTWARE-290 follow-up compared the remaining byte-window validation with recovered Microsoft `VertexBuffer`/`IndexBuffer.CopyData<T>`. XNA first validates the caller array window and stride, then folds signed `offsetInBytes` into the native resource-span calculation; a negative offset therefore fails as `InvalidOperationException` for an invalid resource range, not as a named argument error. CNA explicitly rejected the offset early, and IndexBuffer let it beat `dataIndex`. One shared discriminator failed six of eight exact checks before repair: both mixed-invalid index-buffer calls named `offsetInBytes`, and all four vertex/index Set/Get negative-offset calls exposed `ArgumentOutOfRangeException`. The classic generic paths now preserve Microsoft's copy-window-first order and resource-size failure; raw CNAEXT window operations retain their separately documented argument validation. The complete 48-test buffer slice passes on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL. Full Software graphics passes 2,528/2,592 with the same 64 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-292 | Restore XNA `SetDataOptions` flags and generic dynamic-buffer copy semantics | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** Recovered Microsoft `SetDataOptions` is a `[Flags]` enum, and its native conversion tests bits rather than exact values: `Discard` wins when bit 0 is present, otherwise `NoOverwrite` wins when bit 1 is present, and unknown bits are ignored. CNA exposed no C++ flag operators and compared only exact enumerators, so combined values 3/5/6 were rejected on bound dynamic buffers. The generic `DynamicVertexBuffer.SetData<T>` also entered a raw CNAEXT validation path: null/zero calls became no-ops or reported the wrong priority, `sizeof(T)` was incorrectly required to describe each declared vertex, and it replaced the renderer's fixed declaration stride/count. Two new shared discriminators failed 0/2 before repair across nine exception/option checks; an additional byte-stream discriminator proves eight `float` values may fill two declared 16-byte vertices, preserves the renderer capacity/stride, honors `startIndex`, and lets a short `NoOverwrite` replace only the prefix. Public bitwise operators now model the flags, common buffer uploads canonicalize Microsoft precedence, and fixed-capacity vertex storage separates transfer element width from drawing stride. The stale exact-stride test now asserts Microsoft's byte-capacity failure instead. The complete 28-test buffer slice passes on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL; full Software graphics passes 2,531/2,595 with the same 64 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-293 | Preserve fixed index-buffer capacity and suffix across short native-width uploads | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The generic-width/window routes already composed transfers into the fixed CPU shadow, but the common native-width prefix fast path replaced renderer storage with only `elementCount` entries, resized its logical draw count to the last upload and zero-filled the rest of the public readback shadow. Recovered Microsoft `IndexBuffer.CopyData<T>` locks and copies only the requested byte span within the constructor-fixed `_size`; it never reallocates the resource or changes `IndexCount`. A new shared static/dynamic discriminator failed 0/1 before repair in all four decisive assertions: both untouched suffixes became zero and both four-index renderer counts became two. Native and generic routes now compose one fixed-capacity shadow; full dynamic replacements retain their streaming hint, while shorter/windowed transfers upload the exact complete result without falsely forwarding `NoOverwrite`. A pre-existing test-pinned one-element renderer count was corrected to the fixed four-entry capacity. The focused 10-test slice passes on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL; full Software graphics passes 2,532/2,596 with the same 64 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-294 | Allocate complete classic vertex/index storage at construction | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The adjacent buffer audit found that all four static/dynamic constructors left their shared shadow and renderer count empty until the first `SetData`, even though their public capacity was already fixed. Valid readable `GetData` calls therefore threw `InvalidOperationException`, and an indexed draw was rejected by CNA's client range validation before reaching the renderer. FNA creates each native buffer with exactly `VertexCount * VertexStride` or `IndexCount * indexWidth` bytes in the constructor; recovered Microsoft `CopyData<T>` likewise validates readback against the fixed constructor size and contains no first-upload state. A shared discriminator failed 0/1 before repair with four readback exceptions, four zero renderer counts and one rejected draw. Classic declaration-backed vertex and all index constructors now create a deterministic zeroed shadow and publish the full fixed allocation to the renderer; the CNAEXT empty-declaration convenience constructor remains separate. The 39-test Software buffer slice and matching EasyGL GLES/desktop slices pass completely; full Software graphics passes 2,533/2,597 with the same 64 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-295 | Restore classic `Model` public exception identities | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** A scan for native C++ throws found that the three classic bone-transform copy methods reported an undersized caller array as `std::out_of_range`, while `Model.Draw` reported an Effect not implementing `IEffectMatrices` as `std::runtime_error`. FNA and Microsoft's public contract use `ArgumentOutOfRangeException` with the matching array parameter name and `InvalidOperationException`, respectively. The existing size tests accepted the broad native base class and no test exercised the invalid Effect family; four exact discriminators failed 0/4 before repair. All four paths now expose the XNA exception types. The complete 24-test Software `ModelTest` suite and the focused 4/4 shared EasyGL proof pass. |
| SOFTWARE-296 | Restore unresolved `SpriteFont` glyph exception family | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10 for the exception family; parameter conclusion corrected by SOFTWARE-353.** The native-throw audit found that `SpriteFont.MeasureString` and `SpriteBatch.DrawString` exposed `std::invalid_argument`. FNA explicitly throws `ArgumentException(..., "text")`, which this task followed without then checking Microsoft's higher-authority private glyph resolver. The three original discriminators correctly forced `System::ArgumentException`, but their `text` parameter expectation was wrong. Recovered XNA IL and a runtime probe establish `character`; SOFTWARE-353 supplies that correction and the invalid-default construction/property matrix. |
| SOFTWARE-297 | Restore classic `Texture2D.FromStream` position semantics | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** FNA decodes from the caller's current stream position and, for a seekable stream positioned exactly at its end, rewinds to the beginning first. CNA instead allocated the stream's complete `Length` and demanded that many bytes from the current position; any nonzero prefix over-read at EOF, and the documented rewind case read zero bytes. Two public-overload discriminators failed 0/2 before repair. The shared decoder now rewinds the exact end position and otherwise reads only `Length - Position`, retaining the caller's prefix semantics. The complete eight-test Software format/resize slice passes, and the focused two-test proof passes on EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-298 | Save resolved render-target pixels through classic PNG/JPEG APIs | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** FNA's `SaveAsPng` and `SaveAsJpeg` always read level zero from the live native texture before encoding. CNA instead encoded only `cpuPixels_`, which is deliberately absent for a `RenderTarget2D` because draws mutate renderer-owned storage; both inherited save methods therefore failed every resolved render target without reading one pixel. Two end-to-end clear/save/decode/readback discriminators failed 0/2 before repair. Color saves now obtain encoder-ready bytes through the authoritative public texture readback, preserving ordinary uploads while also inheriting disposed/active-target validation. The complete Software PNG/JPEG family passes 16/16, and both focused round trips pass on EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-299 | Convert every classic surface format for PNG/JPEG saving | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** A measured Microsoft XNA 4.0 probe succeeded for PNG and JPEG across Color, all 16 other classic uncompressed formats and DXT1/3/5, and showed image-save expansion differs from shader sampling: absent color channels become zero, absent alpha becomes one. It also confirmed both encoders clear RGB only at exact zero alpha. CNA instead passed declared-format bytes directly to an RGBA8 encoder, causing wrong output and out-of-bounds reads for one/two-byte texels, while compressed saves had no CPU shadow and failed outright. Five behavioral tests failed 0/5 before repair. A shared internal decoder now produces encoder-ready RGBA8 from authoritative raw level-zero storage, including DXT decompression, signed/packed/float/half conversion and XNA's transparent-pixel normalization. All 21 Software save tests pass, and the focused five-test proof passes on EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL. FNA/FNA3D was deliberately not followed here: its current save path allocates declared-format bytes but its native encoder interprets the pointer as RGBA8, contradicting the measured higher-authority Microsoft behavior. |

CNA instead reused the general pixel-center bilinear scaler and produced interpolated colors. Two public save discriminators failed 0/2 before repair, including exact PNG upscale/downscale pixels and JPEG samples on both sides of the boundary. The save APIs now use an isolated floor-mapped nearest-neighbor helper; `FromStream`, thumbnail and content scaling remain bilinear. Software's complete ImageLoader/save selection passes 26/26, while the direct helper plus both public proofs pass 3/3 on EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL. FNA3D's linear save scaling is a lower-authority divergence from the measured Microsoft contract. |

| SOFTWARE-304 | Restore `Texture2D.FromStream` resize validation and decode-error identity | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** Isolated Microsoft XNA 4.0 probes report `ArgumentOutOfRangeException("width")` before stream decoding for zero/negative width, then the same named height exception for zero/negative height; width wins when both are invalid. | SOFTWARE-305 | Restore XNA `Texture2D.FromStream` seekability validation and precedence | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** Microsoft XNA 4.0 rejects `CanSeek == false` with exact `ArgumentException("stream")` in both overloads before calling `Length`, `Position`, `Read` or `Seek`; that rejection also wins over invalid resize dimensions. | SOFTWARE-307 | Restore Microsoft XNA's classic `Texture2D.FromStream` image-container allowlist | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** An isolated Microsoft XNA 4.0 oracle accepted valid PNG, JPEG and GIF inputs but returned exact `InvalidOperationException` for valid BMP3, TGA, QOI, PSD, HDR and PPM. CNA's unrestricted `stb_image` path decoded every one of those nine inputs, and the existing BMP test incorrectly promoted this implementation detail to a classic compatibility guarantee. FNA independently compiles GIF/PNG/JPEG/TGA/QOI into its ordinary path, so its TGA/QOI extension is lower-authority evidence rather than Microsoft parity. A new shared matrix proves GIF acceptance, independently proves that `ImageLoader` can decode six valid non-XNA containers, and then requires both classic overloads to reject all six. The rejection case failed with twelve missing exceptions before repair. A PNG/JPEG/GIF signature gate now protects only the classic public boundary while leaving the renderer-neutral internal decoder broad. The complete 14-test FromStream family passes on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL. Full graphics passes 2,561/2,625 on Software with 64 classified skips and 2,572/2,641 on EasyGL GLES with 69 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-308 | Prove negative/NPOT bilinear sampler addressing across every U/V mode | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10 with no production mismatch.** The shared sampler corpus had exact negative Wrap/Mirror and mixed-axis coverage only on the point path; its linear checks did not independently prove the address operation around negative tile boundaries. A whole-image analytic oracle now maps a 3x3 NPOT source rectangle spanning two periods on either side of zero through LinearClamp, LinearWrap, LinearMirror and mixed Mirror-U/Wrap-V. Each leg validates all 576 destination pixels, including both bilinear neighbours when they cross a negative boundary, with a narrow two-byte channel allowance for GPU interpolation rounding. The four new legs add eight checks and bring `PointSamplingContract` to 162/162 on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL; every leg reported zero mismatches. This disproves the suspected linear-address implementation gap rather than hiding it behind state round trips. |
| SOFTWARE-309 | Prove active `OcclusionQuery` disposal cannot poison later queries | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10 with no production mismatch.** The 50-iteration active-query destruction stress checked only that destruction did not throw, resource tracking returned to baseline and a later `IsComplete` call itself did not crash; its final assertion was an unconditional `check(true)`, so a native `Begin` failure leaving the new object permanently incomplete remained invisible. The corrected discriminator begins and ends a fresh query after the stress, forces command progress through backbuffer readback, requires the fresh query to complete and requires `PixelCount` to become available. Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop compiled-effect EasyGL all pass. This closes the lifecycle evidence blind spot and disproves the suspected EasyGL target-poisoning gap. |
| SOFTWARE-310 | Make a zero-area EasyGL scissor reject every fragment | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The renderer-neutral deferred-scissor fixture explicitly declared EasyGL's zero-width/height behavior as an accepted renderer divergence. EasyGL instead returned early for `w <= 0 || h <= 0`, retaining the preceding rectangle and drawing through seven of nine exact black probes. Correcting the test produced the expected pre-fix 49/50 result on both EasyGL GLES and desktop GL while Software remained 50/50. EasyGL now forwards zero extents and retains only a defensive negative guard; both profiles pass 50/50, including target/backbuffer, deferred/immediate, SpriteBatch, every stock effect, viewport interaction and state transitions. |

EasyGL now temporarily disables and restores scissor testing across all seven renderer clear entry points. The shared fixture adds independent depth-only and stencil-only discriminators in addition to color and post-clear state-restoration coverage; final results are Software 45/45 and both EasyGL profiles 51/51 on an isolated virtual display. The separate DirectX9 backend's raw-state behavior is identified accurately in the test but is outside this Software/EasyGL implementation task. |

EasyGL GLES and desktop pass the identical 17/17 contract under an isolated virtual display. The obsolete Additive commentary now records the repair rather than contradicting its own assertion. |
| SOFTWARE-315 | Apply `RasterizerState.MultiSampleAntiAlias` to classic lines and wireframe edges | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** `SOFTWARE-160` propagated the public state and made triangle coverage observable, but Software's `LineList`/`LineStrip` and solid-triangle wireframe routes still used a whole-pixel DDA that wrote every enabled sample under both state values. FNA3D maps the state to global `GL_MULTISAMPLE`, its D3D11 driver writes `RasterizerStateDesc.MultisampleEnable`, and desktop EasyGL measured the missing distinction directly: a horizontal 4x line produced 12 partial pixels when true and none when false, while pre-fix Software produced zero partial pixels and identical images for both. The CPU line walker now evaluates a one-pixel segment footprint at the same four quarter-pixel locations as triangles and sends the resulting mask through alpha/depth/stencil/query/blend/color stages; false retains one pixel-center decision replicated to all samples. The shared contract covers triangle, `LineList` and wireframe true/false/true transitions: Software and desktop EasyGL pass 26/26, and GLES passes all 17 representable checks while retaining its documented inability to disable multisample rasterization. Seven related Software raster/wireframe/clipping/depth/stencil/topology tests pass after relinking. |
| SOFTWARE-316 | Evaluate line and wireframe depth independently at every covered MSAA sample | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-10.** The `SOFTWARE-315` coverage repair still sent one pixel-center depth to all covered line samples. A depth-gradient `LineList` followed by a constant-depth quad exposed the omission without relying on exact line footprint: desktop EasyGL retained one of two covered green samples in a boundary pixel (`G=64`), while pre-fix Software retained either both (`G=127`) or neither. The CPU line walker now returns the original-segment interpolation parameter at each covered sample; ordinary lines and both colored/shaded wireframe consumers derive separate post-divide depths, including polygon bias, and pass them through the existing per-sample depth/stencil/query pipeline. Permanent line and wireframe discriminators first require a two-sample coverage pixel and then require the constant-depth overlay to leave exactly one green sample, preventing footprint differences or one-sample endpoints from satisfying the check. Software and desktop EasyGL pass 28/28; EasyGL GLES passes all 19 representable checks. The related Software clipping, wireframe, depth-bias and stencil-matrix regressions pass. |

The AlphaTest leg now supplies its deliberate white texture and a proper `VertexPositionTexture` stream; the dedicated `AlphaTestEffectContract` continues to require opaque-black null sampling. The complete shared fog matrix passes 33/33 on Software, EasyGL GLES and desktop EasyGL. |

Its DXT1 DDS storage/draw and DXT5 XNB paths pass 3/3; after SOFTWARE-317/318 the fully relinked Software CTest label returns to 158/158. |

A shared 8x8 boundary discriminator proved the output mismatch: EasyGL resolved one green sample (`G=64`) while pre-fix Software resolved none. The expanded shared contract passes 29/29 on Software and desktop EasyGL; GLES passes all 20 representable checks. Fifteen sensitive Software clipping, fill, depth, stencil, MSAA, wireframe, strip and cube-target tests pass, followed by the complete 158/158 Software label and 2,561/2,625 full graphics suite with the same 64 classified skips. |
| SOFTWARE-320 | Preserve FNA/XNA vertex-usage collision remapping across bound streams | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11.** FNA3D's D3D11, OpenGL and SDL drivers walk every element of every binding in slot order; a repeated `(VertexElementUsage, UsageIndex)` is assigned the first free index of that usage while unique elements in the same stream remain live. CNA instead discarded an entirely colliding stream and threw `NotSupportedException` for a partially colliding stream. The full Software label passes 158/158 and `CnaGraphicsTests` passes 2,561/2,625 with the same 64 classified skips; desktop EasyGL passes 2,573/2,641 with 68 classified skips, and its full enabled compiled-effect family passes 39/39, including the multi-stream and instancing controls. |

Recovered XNA draw IL contains no managed vertex-capacity check; FNA likewise forwards all bindings and its stock HLSL varies the native input declaration with effect properties. The common binding tuple now marks shader-consumed streams after SOFTWARE-320 remapping. Software pointer formation/decoding and EasyGL stock VAO binding skip entirely inactive streams. At task closure the focused active-short cases still expected CNA's inherited range exception; SOFTWARE-322 later corrected that last premise without weakening the inactive-stream proof. Folding a CNA binding offset now saturates through 64-bit arithmetic rather than invoking signed-overflow UB. | SOFTWARE-325 | Restore Microsoft XNA fallback behavior for invalid state-object enum values | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming a shared classic-XNA conformance gap.** Recovered Microsoft XNA accepts cast/unknown enum values in state-object setters and converts them at native application: `Blend`→Zero, `BlendFunction`→Add, `CompareFunction`→Always, `StencilOperation`→Keep, `CullMode`→None, `FillMode`→Solid, `TextureFilter`→Linear and `TextureAddressMode`→Wrap. | SOFTWARE-326 | Preserve the low-eight-bit XNA `ReferenceStencil` contract across renderers | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming an EasyGL/shared-plumbing classic-XNA gap.** Recovered XNA accepts every signed `Int32`, casts it to the D3D9 DWORD stencil reference and retains the original public property. A diagnostic rendered opposite results: Software already used the effective low byte (`-1`→255, `256`→0), while EasyGL passed the signed integer to OpenGL and inherited its saturating reference clamp (`-1`→0, `256`→255). The shared GraphicsDevice boundary now normalizes only renderer-facing whole-state and independent-device values to eight bits while preserving the caller-visible `Int32`. | SOFTWARE-337 | Preserve `SpriteBatch.layerDepth` in EasyGL vertex rasterization | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming an EasyGL classic-XNA gap hidden by default-state tests.** FNA writes `layerDepth` into `POSITION0.Z` for all four sprite vertices and applies the caller's `DepthStencilState`. | SOFTWARE-338 | Audit and restore full `SpriteBatch` transform-matrix Z/W semantics in Software | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming a classic-XNA Software gap.** FNA generates `(x,y,layerDepth,1)` before multiplying by the complete SpriteBatch matrix. | SOFTWARE-339 | Restore Microsoft XNA invalid `SpriteSortMode` flush behavior | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming a shared classic-XNA error-semantics gap.** Valid sort-mode tests could not expose CNA's implicit default: any unknown enum value queued sprites like a sorted mode but `flushBatch()` skipped every sorter and submitted them in Deferred order. Recovered Microsoft XNA 4.0 IL shows `Flush()` calls `SortSprites()` for every nonzero mode and its switch accepts only Texture/BackToFront/FrontToBack, otherwise throwing parameterless `NotSupportedException`; an empty queue never enters `Flush()`. A focused pre-fix test therefore observed no exception and one incorrectly submitted draw. The shared SpriteBatch layer now throws before any draw for an unknown nonzero mode while preserving the empty-queue no-sort case. The complete ten-mode plus End-exception selection passes 11/11 in displayless Software and EasyGL builds. |
| SOFTWARE-340 | Preserve signed and zero SpriteBatch source extents in Software | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming a Software-only classic-XNA geometry gap.** Microsoft XNA's recovered `InternalDraw` stores the source width/height, destination and origin without clamping; FNA's equivalent algebra and EasyGL likewise preserve the signed source extent. Software alone replaced each nonpositive source dimension with one before computing origin-relative corners. With source `(2,0,-2,2)` and origin `(1,0)`, both vector-position/scalar-scale and rectangle-destination overloads therefore drew on the opposite side of the pivot. Both exact pixel discriminators failed 0/2 pre-fix on Software and passed 2/2 on EasyGL GLES. | SOFTWARE-341 | Compute SpriteBatch source endpoints without signed-integer overflow | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming a shared classic-XNA correctness gap.** Software and EasyGL both formed the far UV endpoint as `float(source.X + source.Width)`, invoking signed-overflow undefined behavior before conversion. Recovered Microsoft XNA stores source origin and extent as separate `Single` fields; FNA likewise normalizes them separately and adds them only in float-domain sprite expansion. An isolated Microsoft XNA 4.0 Reach oracle rendered every pixel yellow when PointClamp drew a 2x2 red/green/blue/yellow texture with source `(INT_MAX,INT_MAX,1,1)`; both pre-fix CNA renderers instead selected red. Both now add the separately converted normalized extent. Because Mesa GLES also resolves very large positive hardware sampler coordinates to the opposite edge, EasyGL reduces an already-constant, wholly out-of-range Clamp coordinate to its equivalent edge only for the stock SpriteEffect; equal endpoints preserve mip derivatives and custom Effects retain their original coordinates. | SOFTWARE-342 | Preserve exact SpriteBatch screen coordinates through homogeneous clipping | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after a full point-sampling rerun exposed a regression from SOFTWARE-338.** The new homogeneous route projected every already-transformed SpriteBatch corner into clip space and expanded it through the viewport again. Although algebraically inverse, finite `Single` arithmetic moved an exact screen edge from `x=4.5` to about `4.5000005`; the top-left test then rejected all 14 pixel centers on that edge. The pre-existing anisotropic transform case consequently failed 161/162 despite the narrower SOFTWARE-338 Z/W tests passing. `ClipVertex` now carries the original viewport-local homogeneous SpriteBatch X/Y through the same six-plane clipper; the final sprite conversion divides those preserved values by W and adds the viewport origin directly. This retains exact W=1 placement while keeping clipped/varying-W perspective semantics. | SOFTWARE-343 | Measure the complete real-XNA oracle corpus on Software | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 with an exhaustive renderer-neutral corpus measurement.** The shared oracle executable is now a permanent Software target and a displayless script renders every one of the 39 checked-in Microsoft XNA 4.0 reference scenes before reporting exact/tolerated pixel differences without turning a hardware-sensitive image comparison into a portable CTest gate. Software rendered all 39 scenes: 8/39 are byte-exact, 26/39 match at a one-byte channel tolerance and 28/39 at two bytes. All stock lighting, fog, environment-map and skinning scenes except one point-boundary lit-texture case fall within one byte. Seven AlphaTest modes plus the ordinary and lit textured quads differ on the same exact interpolated point-texel threshold; separate constant-UV and binary-boundary real-XNA probes match Software byte-exactly and disprove a sampler tie-rule defect, leaving D3D9 interpolation precision rather than a semantic sampler gap. Rotated/flipped sprites have only eight two-byte rounding pixels. The remaining two line scenes expose a reproducible coverage mismatch and are separated into `SOFTWARE-344` rather than hidden by tolerance. |
| SOFTWARE-344 | Reproduce and reconcile classic non-MSAA line coverage with Microsoft XNA | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after confirming a Software-only classic-XNA rasterization gap.** The full corpus recorded two wrong `LineList` endpoint pixels and 76 `LineStrip` pixels: the generic ceil/floor DDA included ending diamonds the line never exited and shifted fractional diagonal coverage inward. Direct3D 9 specifies the non-antialiased GDI line rule; Microsoft's GDI documentation defines inclusive/exclusive endpoints, and the later D3D functional specification makes the equivalent directed diamond-exit test explicit. Software now clips the segment parametrically against each candidate pixel's four half-pixel diamond planes, accepts only an exit before the ending vertex and visits only five bounded neighbours per dominant-axis coordinate. The two checked 256x256 XNA scenes improve from 2/76 differences to byte-exact 408/408 and 307/307 coverage. Five additional isolated Microsoft XNA 4.0 probes prove horizontal and exact-diagonal reversal plus a sub-diamond short segment byte-exactly; current EasyGL matches all five without a production change. Both line scenes are permanent exact Software/EasyGL CTests. Nine sensitive clipping, stencil, MSAA, SpriteBatch, wireframe and depth-bias CTests pass, as do five additional stencil/indexed regressions; full Software graphics passes 2,610/2,673 with the same 63 classified skips. SOFTWARE-346 subsequently separates varying evaluation from that coverage location. |
| SOFTWARE-345 | Restore XNA/D3D round-to-nearest RGBA8 render-target quantization | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after the line-interpolation challenge exposed a Software-only Color-target defect.** `SoftwareFramebuffer::ToUnorm8` truncated `value * 255`, although the same framebuffer already rounded every 10- and 16-bit UNORM format and real Microsoft XNA rendered exact half-byte gradient values upward. A direct storage guard now pins 0.5→128, 64.5/255→65 and 190.5/255→191. SOFTWARE-344 reused that displaced coverage point for line varyings, making exact half-byte values one byte low even after correct UNORM conversion. A `RasterVertex` now carries the logical interpolation offset independently: ordinary Software 3D evaluates at `pixel+63/128` against equally shifted endpoints, while SpriteBatch and multisampled geometry retain `pixel+0.5`; coverage diamonds remain unchanged. Microsoft XNA's 64x64 red-to-blue horizontal and 45-degree lines now match Software at every byte (0/4096 deltas each, formerly two each after SOFTWARE-345); the public viewport/LineList test pins five endpoint/midpoint values plus endpoint exclusion and passes in the 57/57 wireframe contract. A fractional line retains 34 one-byte floating-point deltas and is not misreported as exact. Six sensitive clipping, MSAA, wireframe, depth-bias and oracle CTests pass. |
| SOFTWARE-347 | Test whether the classic-line varying correction applies to triangle interpolation | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 by disproving the tempting universal correction.** Applying the line's `pixel+63/128` varying point to both triangle raster paths changed the checked XNA `colored3d` delta from 1,130 to 1,049 one-byte pixels, but that net count concealed 261 repaired pixels and 180 newly broken exact pixels. Most decisively, 29 sampled pixels across row `y=72` already matched XNA's green value 203; the alternative changed every one to 204. The wider corpus was similarly mixed: several colored/lit scenes improved while EnvironmentMap and skinned variants regressed. D3D9's fixed-function subpixel/interpolator precision therefore cannot be modeled by transplanting the aliased-line rule. The experiment was reverted; current triangle residuals stay classified as one-byte hardware precision, not a proven semantic gap. |
| SOFTWARE-348 | Preserve `VertexBuffer.VertexDeclaration` resource semantics across C++ wrappers | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after executable Microsoft XNA evidence confirmed the shared Core gap recorded by SOFTWARE-207.** An isolated XNA 4.0 probe returned the same object, bound both views to the target device, shared later Name/Tag mutations and disposal, and raised one source-sender event. The pre-fix CNA discriminator failed device binding, metadata, lifetime and event observations because the buffer kept an independent value copy. `GraphicsResource` now offers an opt-in shared identity used by `VertexDeclaration`: aliases share device/lifetime/metadata, survive safe wrapper moves and scope exits, and notify every wrapper-local subscriber when the managed-equivalent identity is explicitly disposed. `VertexBuffer` binds that identity and exposes mutable/const property views. Microsoft decompilation additionally shows declarations use a separate binding manager rather than the general device resource manager, so device disposal releases the binding without disposing the declaration object. Five focused tests cover the complete semantic matrix, source-wrapper lifetime, cross-device ownership, non-disposing device teardown and reusable built-in declarations across completed C++ device scopes; the 98-test declaration/buffer slice passes on both renderers. Full graphics passes 2,615/2,678 with 63 classified skips on displayless Software and 2,609/2,678 with 69 classified skips on isolated EasyGL GLES. Literal caller-address/event-token identity remains SOFTWARE-207 rather than being faked with a dangling borrowed pointer. |
| SOFTWARE-349 | Preserve graphics-state `GraphicsResource` semantics across public aliases and preset defaults | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after the SOFTWARE-198 follow-up produced direct pre-fix failures.** Microsoft XNA's recovered `Apply` implementations retain the assigned state object and replace its parent device; its `GraphicsDevice` constructor assigns the three named preset objects, while both XNA/FNA sampler collections fill every slot with the same `LinearWrap` reference. CNA shared only the property/disposal payload: source and device/slot wrappers still reported null devices, later `Name`/`Tag` mutations diverged, base `IsDisposed` and disposal events were independent, and default presets/slots were deep copies. All four state families now opt into the shared `GraphicsResource` identity at assignment, bind that identity to the applying device, retain it safely after source scope exit and rebind it across devices. Device defaults share their named preset identities and all untouched sampler slots share `LinearWrap`. The device lifetime token is declared before state collections so constructor-time sampler binding cannot observe unconstructed storage. Three focused tests cover custom/default metadata, tag, device, disposal/event, source lifetime, preset/slot aliasing and cross-device behavior; the 177-test state slice passes on displayless Software and isolated EasyGL GLES. Final full graphics passes 2,618/2,681 with 63 skips on Software and 2,612/2,681 with 69 skips on EasyGL. Literal caller-address/event-token identity remains SOFTWARE-198 rather than being faked. |
| SOFTWARE-350 | Restore active disposed-state reference no-op and dirty reapply semantics | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after recovered Microsoft control flow invalidated SOFTWARE-234's same-state assertion.** An isolated Microsoft XNA 4.0 probe proves that reassigning each already-active disposed Blend/DepthStencil/Rasterizer/Sampler object succeeds, assigning that sampler to a different slot throws `ObjectDisposedException`, and drawing with all four already-applied disposed states still succeeds. Assigning even the current `BlendFactor` or `ReferenceStencil` first dirties the associated cache, after which same-reference state reassignment reaches `Apply` and throws. CNA instead called `BindForUse` before every identity comparison; eight new no-op assertions failed pre-fix. Different disposed states remain rejected, and cross-device tests distinguish cached-property/slot no-op from a different-slot parent rebind. Full graphics passes 2,619/2,682 with 63 skips on Software and 2,613/2,682 with 69 skips on EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-351 | Restore Microsoft SpriteBatch device-wide Begin/End accounting and deferred-failure state | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after direct Microsoft XNA 4.0 IL and an isolated runtime probe overturned SOFTWARE-189/218's FNA-first conclusion.** XNA stores `spriteBeginCount` and `spriteImmediateBeginCount` on GraphicsDevice: multiple Deferred batches may coexist, but Immediate rejects any active batch and every non-Immediate mode rejects an active Immediate batch. Failed Immediate state application publishes neither counter nor pair. Deferred state/flush failure leaves the same batch active, makes repeated Begin fail, makes repeated End retry, and keeps Immediate blocked even if the poisoned batch is disposed. CNA had no device counters and cleared `begun` before End work. Shared counters now publish only after a successful Begin setup and release only after successful common End work. Renderer-private End failure remains recoverable because that CNA seam cannot safely retry a native pair. Four pre-fix regressions failed; the 78-test SpriteBatch/format slice passes on both renderers. Full graphics passes 2,621/2,684 with 63 classified skips on displayless Software and 2,615/2,684 with 69 classified skips on isolated EasyGL GLES. |

Recovered XNA `InternalDraw` IL and FNA's `PushSprite` store the values directly with no range check. An isolated Microsoft XNA 4.0 probe accepts `float.MaxValue` and `-float.MaxValue` for both position and scale in Immediate and Deferred modes, and both `End` calls succeed. Five of seven corrected numeric-input tests failed before the production change. Draw and DrawString now preserve the complete `Single` domain; the legacy integer-only renderer fallback saturates finite overflow and maps non-finite inputs to zero so unsupported implementations cannot trigger C++ float-to-int undefined behavior. A real-renderer test additionally submits an extreme off-screen sprite followed by a visible one. The focused 9/9 and wider 78/78 SpriteBatch selections pass on both renderers. Full graphics passes 2,622/2,685 with 63 classified skips on displayless Software and 2,616/2,685 with 69 classified skips on isolated EasyGL GLES. |
| SOFTWARE-353 | Restore Microsoft SpriteFont default-character construction and lookup semantics | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after recovered IL and an isolated XNA 4.0 runtime probe disproved both CNA and FNA.** Microsoft's internal content constructor stores `defaultCharacter` directly even when it is absent from the glyph map; its public setter separately validates and throws unparameterized `ArgumentException` without changing the old value. `GetIndexForCharacter` recursively resolves the default and reports `ArgumentException("character")` when neither requested nor fallback glyph exists. FNA instead accepts the public setter and reports `text` or a dictionary failure; CNA rejected construction, named the setter parameter `value`, and copied FNA's `text`/`KeyNotFoundException` lookup behavior. The shared SpriteFont resolver now follows Microsoft and is used by MeasureString plus every DrawString overload. All seven authoritative tests failed before repair. Full graphics passes 2,624/2,687 with 63 classified skips on Software and 2,618/2,687 with 69 on EasyGL. |
| SOFTWARE-354 | Restore Microsoft XNA SpriteBatch sort algorithm and unordered-depth behavior | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after direct Microsoft XNA 4.0 evidence overturned another FNA-derived assumption.** Recovered XNA IL fills an index array and calls `.NET Framework 4 Array.Sort<int>` for Texture, FrontToBack and BackToFront. Its depth comparers use bare ordered comparisons, making `NaN` compare equal to every value; its framework quicksort is unstable and reverses three equal keys. CNA instead used `std::stable_sort` plus FNA's total float order, preserving equal sprites and gathering every `NaN` at one end. Isolated XNA probes pin all six three-depth permutations, 64 mixed finite/NaN/infinity entries, and visible equal-key overlap. Three CNA discriminators failed 0/3 before repair. The shared layer now reproduces the framework quicksort explicitly, avoiding the undefined behavior of passing XNA's non-transitive NaN comparator to a standard C++ sort. Texture grouping uses the same unstable path, and the stale unknown-enum documentation now matches SOFTWARE-339. The complete 141-test SpriteBatch/SpriteFont slice passes on displayless Software and isolated EasyGL GLES; full graphics passes 2,625/2,688 with 63 classified skips on Software and 2,619/2,688 with 69 on EasyGL. |

| SOFTWARE-371 | Execute compiled `ps_3_0` `VPOS`/`VFACE` raster inputs | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after falsifying both renderers against the classic ps_3_0 contract.** The active MojoShader/EasyGL GLSL profile accepts both miscellaneous inputs, but Software's decoded program dropped their declarations and its pixel machine rejected register family 17. The first Software draw failed with `unsupported source register type 17`. EasyGL executed the same real Effect but exposed OpenGL half-centres (the 4x4 coordinate ramp quantized to 32/96/159/223 instead of 0/64/128/191) and reversed the XNA face sign, making clockwise triangles blue zero and counter-clockwise triangles blue 255. Software now retains both declarations, accepts explicit per-fragment built-ins in scalar and 2x2-quad execution, publishes integer-centred top-down coordinates and a positive clockwise/front triangle sign, and propagates the originating face through wireframe edges without promising `VFACE` for standalone line/point primitives where Microsoft defines it as undefined. The managed MojoShader patch floors translated `gl_FragCoord.xy`; an opt-in face-flip uniform lets EasyGL map its unchanged counter-clockwise GL convention to XNA while leaving FNA3D callers' default unchanged. One shared 4x4 behavioral fixture renders both windings and checks every exact RGBA pixel. The focused displayless Software runtime passes, and all 44 enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. [Microsoft ps_3_0 registers](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-ps-3-0) and [D3D9 semantics](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx-graphics-hlsl-semantics) define the recovered coordinate/facing contract. |
