Because the test only required opposite tuples, those reversed names let Software's `area < 0` selection and EasyGL's `CCW -> GL_BACK` installation pass. [Microsoft's D3D9 state contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d9/d3drenderstatetype) is explicit: clockwise triangles use `D3DRS_STENCIL*` and counter-clockwise triangles use `D3DRS_CCW_STENCIL*`; its [two-sided-stencil contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3d9/two-sided-stencil) says the mode is ignored for lines and point sprites. Applying only the corrected CCW fixture failed identically on both renderers: the two-sided column stayed background while the control passed. Software now selects the CCW tuple for positive area in colored, stock-effect and compiled-effect triangles while retaining the ordinary tuple for non-triangles. EasyGL assigns CCW function/fail/depth-fail/pass/write-mask/reference updates to `GL_FRONT` under its unchanged `GL_CCW` convention and the ordinary tuple to `GL_BACK`; immediately before a non-triangle draw it installs the ordinary tuple on both GL faces, then restores the split for the next triangle even when the public state object did not change. The focused two-sided and `ReferenceStencil` tests pass on both; the expanded shared matrix passes 46/46 on both and independently exercises all four CCW properties, the CW ordinary tuple, the line exception and line-to-triangle restoration. |
| SOFTWARE-373 | Execute Shader Model 3 instruction-token destination predication | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after a valid classic Effect falsified both renderers.** [Microsoft's predicate-register contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-predicate) defines `p0` as four component Booleans, and [`setp_comp`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/setp-comp---ps) allows an arithmetic or texture instruction's destination components to be masked by identity/replicate `p0` with optional `!`. Software explicitly threw `predicated instructions require SOFTWARE-165`; the pinned MojoShader GLSL profile independently rejected the same valid bytecode with `predicated destinations unsupported` and incorrectly classified pixel `p0` as scalar. The managed MojoShader patch retains vector `p0`, evaluates the result once, snapshots the predicate before a predicated `SETP` can overwrite it and conditionally assigns the requested scalar/vector components with the correct float/int/bool type. At this task boundary the special non-writing `TEXKILL` case remained unproven; SOFTWARE-374 subsequently closes it. `CompiledEffects=false` therefore remains honest. |
| SOFTWARE-374 | Honor Shader Model predication on `TEXKILL` | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after the follow-up to SOFTWARE-373 exposed a distinct EasyGL emitter hole.** Microsoft's [`setp_comp` predication contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/setp-comp---ps) permits any arithmetic or texture instruction to be controlled component-by-component, while [`TEXKILL`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/texkill---ps) discards when any of its first three components is negative. MojoShader parsed a predicated `TEXKILL` but its GLSL emitter read only the kill operand and discarded unconditionally; the new two-state real Effect therefore returned the magenta clear instead of lime when `p0.x` was false. The managed patch snapshots the identity/replicate/optionally-negated vector predicate and includes each x/y/z predicate component in the corresponding negative-value test. Software's existing component mask already followed that rule but had no proof. The shared fixture now proves false `p0.x` suppresses kill and true `p0.x` executes it; Software's focused runtime and all 46 enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass in isolated Xvfb. |

The focused Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests, the full 2,625/2,688 Software graphics suite with 63 classified skips, and all 67 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-395 | Restore D3D9 `NRM` fixed-XYZ and zero-vector semantics in Software and EasyGL | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after both compiled paths failed the official three-component rule.** Microsoft's pixel and vertex contracts always derive the reciprocal length from source XYZ, then multiply all four source components by that same factor; a zero XYZ vector uses finite `FLT_MAX`. Both Software machines instead derived length from the destination write mask, so a full write incorrectly included W and an XY write became a two-dimensional normalization. MojoShader made the same error by passing its destination-masked source to GLSL `normalize()`. Direct Software probes prove full-write W exclusion, partial-write XYZ inclusion and the zero-XYZ W result. A real ps_2_0 Effect combines an XY mask over `(3,4,12,26)` with a W-only zero-XYZ probe: pre-fix Software and EasyGL emitted black, while the corrected fixed-XYZ paths emit exact white. The managed GLSL patch preserves source modifiers/swizzles, computes one XYZ factor and applies the destination mask only after normalization. The focused Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests, the full 2,625/2,688 Software graphics suite with 63 classified skips, and all 68 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-396 | Restore Shader Model 1.1 implicit vertex inputs and legacy `EXPP` semantics | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after the next profile-level discriminator falsified both compiled paths and exposed an additional Software-only prerequisite.** Shader Model 1 vertex programs have no `dcl` instructions: MojoShader derives their fixed v0..v16 usage map, but Software only built input bindings from explicit declarations, so every parsed `vs_1_1` input retained its default value. Separately, [Microsoft's `EXPP` contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/expp---vs) requires vs_1_1 to emit `(2^floor(v), v-floor(v), 2^v, 1)` while Shader Model 2+ replicates `2^v`; both Software and EasyGL/MojoShader used the later form for every profile. Software now imports the parser's implicit attributes through the fixed D3D9 register map and executes the legacy tuple only for major version one. A managed MojoShader GLSL patch applies the same version split. Independent fixtures prove a plain declaration-free v0/POSITION0 transform and exact CPU tuple components; a real parsed Effect gates full-quad coverage on all four tuple values and failed both renderers before repair. The focused Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests, the full 2,625/2,688 graphics suite with 63 classified skips, and all 70 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-397 | Correct the complete Shader Model 1.1 fixed vertex-input map | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 when reviewing SOFTWARE-396's new inverse map against the actual MojoShader/D3D9 table exposed an immediate conformance bug.** The first fixture proved only v0/POSITION0. Software's inverse incorrectly assigned TEXCOORD0..7 to v5..v12, invented TANGENT/BINORMAL aliases and omitted COLOR entirely; the fixed map is v5/v6 = COLOR0/1 and v7..v14 = TEXCOORD0..7, followed by POSITION1/NORMAL1. A second declaration-free `vs_1_1` now consumes v5 and the boundary v14 together. Before repair, Software rejected the parsed Effect because COLOR0 had no mapping; the corrected parser binds both semantics and preserves a full quad. The direct Software runtime and all 71 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-398 | Reject temporary self-read before initialization in shared MojoShader validation | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-11 after an instruction-order discriminator falsified both compiled paths.** Microsoft's pixel-shader register contract requires shader creation to fail when a temporary is read before it has been written. MojoShader marked an instruction destination initialized before parsing that instruction's sources, so an initial `mov r0, r0` incorrectly validated even though no earlier instruction had defined `r0`; Software inherited the same parser result. A synthetic otherwise-valid ps_2_0 Effect was accepted by both Software and EasyGL before repair. The managed parser patch now remembers whether the current destination existed before the instruction and rejects a same-register temporary source when it did not, while preserving legal read/modify/write instructions after initialization. The direct Software parser/runtime test, all 160 Software CTests and all 72 dependency-enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass, the last on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-399 | Track component initialization for `TEXKILL` | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after following SOFTWARE-398 into MojoShader's adjacent explicit FIXME.** Microsoft's [temporary-register contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-temporary) requires every component actually read by a pixel instruction to have been defined. The parser nevertheless tracked only a register-wide Boolean and treated destination-coded `TEXKILL` as a write, so a ps_2_0 Effect that wrote only `r0.x` before `texkill r0` was accepted by both Software and EasyGL. The managed parser began accumulating destination write masks, rejecting incomplete `TEXKILL` temporaries and restoring the prior mask because kill is read-only. Its initial control wrote all XYZW; SOFTWARE-454's subsequent Microsoft matrix established that this was over-broad, narrowed the real `ps_1_4`/`ps_2_0` requirement to XYZ and added the other profile/input rules. The component-tracking prerequisite introduced here remains. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 74 then-current dependency-enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests passed. General per-opcode component-read validation remains part of SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-400 | Restore D3D9 `SGN` profile and scratch-register validation | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after four independent profile discriminators exposed inverted shared validation.** Microsoft's [`sgn` vertex contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/sgn---vs) defines `src1/src2` as two different temporary scratch registers whose incoming values are not part of the sign computation, and the pixel instruction tables do not expose `SGN`. MojoShader instead parsed both scratch operands as ordinary reads, accepted arbitrary/aliased register types and advertised the opcode to pixel shaders. Before repair, Software and EasyGL rejected the valid uninitialized-scratch vertex Effect, accepted constant scratch registers and accepted pixel `SGN`; aliasing happened to fail only because the same wrong uninitialized-read check fired first. A dedicated parser form now skips value-read validation only for the two scratch operands, while semantic validation requires distinct temporaries and the opcode table restricts `SGN` to vertex shaders. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 78 dependency-enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass, the last on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-401 | Restore D3D9 `EXPP`/`LOGP` stage and scalar-source validation | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after three parser discriminators failed on both compiled paths.** Microsoft's [`EXPP`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/expp---vs) and [`LOGP`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/logp---vs) contracts expose the partial-precision instructions only to vertex profiles and require an explicit replicate source swizzle. MojoShader instead advertised both opcodes to either stage and gave only `LOGP` a replicate check. Otherwise-valid ps_2_0 `EXPP` and `LOGP` Effects and a vs_2_0 `EXPP c0.xyzw` Effect were therefore accepted by Software and EasyGL before repair. The managed parser patch restricts both opcodes to vertex shaders and gives `EXPP` the same scalar-source validation as `LOGP`. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 81 dependency-enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass, the last on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-402 | Restore vertex-only `LIT`/`SLT`/`SGE` and scalar `EXP` validation | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after five independent parser discriminators failed on both compiled paths.** Microsoft's [`LIT`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/lit---vs), [`SLT`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/slt---vs) and [`SGE`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/sge---vs) contracts expose those arithmetic opcodes only to vertex profiles, while the pixel Shader Model 2/3 instruction tables omit them; Microsoft's pixel and vertex [`EXP`](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/exp---ps) contracts require an explicit replicate source swizzle. MojoShader instead advertised all four opcodes to either stage and performed no `EXP` swizzle validation, so Software and EasyGL accepted pixel `LIT`/`SLT`/`SGE` and both pixel and vertex `EXP c0.xyzw`. The audit also found that the earlier NRM and signed-LOG behavioral fixtures themselves encoded illegal pixel `SLT`/`SGE`; replacing their gates with legal `ADD`/`CMP` sequences demonstrates why a green shared suite was not conformance proof. The managed parser patch restricts `LIT`/`SLT`/`SGE` to vertex shaders and validates scalar `EXP`. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 86 dependency-enabled EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass, the last on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. Broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-403 | Enforce the Direct3D 9 pixel Shader Model 1.x opcode allowlist | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the profile audit found a broad shared acceptance hole.** Microsoft's complete [`ps_1_1`–`ps_1_4` instruction table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-ps-1-x) permits only the small legacy arithmetic/texture set and records the minor-version differences. MojoShader instead exposed nearly every generally typed opcode to `ps_1_x`; EasyGL accepted all thirteen well-formed `ps_1_4` probes for `RCP`, `RSQ`, `MIN`, `MAX`, `EXP`, `LOG`, `FRC`, `POW`, `CRS`, `ABS`, `NRM`, `DSX` and `DSY`, while Software accepted seven and happened to reject six only in later renderer-specific decoding. The shared parser now uses an explicit complete Model 1 pixel allowlist, including `DP4`/`CMP`, legacy texture-matrix/remap and `BEM` minor-version boundaries, before parsing or emitting an instruction. The thirteen negative probes all pass; existing positive ps_1_2/1_3/1_4 execution remains green in the complete 99-test EasyGL compiled family. The direct Software runtime and all 160 Software CTests pass. Broader vertex/SM2/SM3 profile validation and lifecycle/content closure remain SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-404 | Enforce the Direct3D 9 vertex Shader Model 1.1 opcode allowlist | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the adjacent profile audit exposed another broad shared parser hole.** Microsoft's complete [`vs_1_1` instruction table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-vs-instructions-vs-1-1) is a small explicit set: it includes the legacy arithmetic and matrix instructions plus `DCL`, `DEF`, `EXP`, `LOG`, `EXPP` and `LOGP`, but not `LRP` or later-profile arithmetic, address and typed-constant instructions. MojoShader had no corresponding version gate. EasyGL therefore accepted all nine well-formed `vs_1_1` probes for `ABS`, `CRS`, `NRM`, `POW`, `SINCOS`, `SGN`, `MOVA`, `DEFB` and `DEFI`; Software accepted one and happened to reject the other eight only in its later decoder. The shared parser now applies the complete allowlist before parsing or emitting an instruction. All nine negative probes, the direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and the complete 108-test isolated EasyGL compiled family pass. Broader SM2/SM3 profile validation and lifecycle/content closure remain SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-405 | Enforce the Direct3D 9 pixel Shader Model 2.0 opcode allowlist | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the next complete profile comparison proved a shared acceptance hole.** Microsoft's [`ps_2_0` instruction table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-ps-2-0) contains arithmetic, declarations and the basic `TEXLD` family, but no typed constants, flow control, derivatives, explicit-gradient/level sampling or predication; those arrive in `ps_2_x` or `ps_3_0`. MojoShader nevertheless exposed its generic Shader Model 2+ table to exact `ps_2_0`. Before repair, Software and EasyGL both accepted all seven well-formed probes for `DEFB`, `DEFI`, `REP`, `IF`, `DSX`, `DSY` and `SETP`. The shared parser now applies the complete exact-2.0 allowlist before argument parsing or emission, while leaving `ps_2_x`, software profiles and `ps_3_0` untouched. All seven negative probes, the direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and the complete 115-test isolated EasyGL compiled family pass; existing ps_2_0 draw fixtures provide positive coverage for the retained instructions. Broader Shader Model 2.x/3 profile validation and lifecycle/content closure remain SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-406 | Enforce Shader Model 2.0 dynamic-flow and predication boundaries | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the exact vertex-profile comparison exposed a second Shader Model 2 acceptance hole.** Microsoft's [`vs_2_0` instruction table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-vs-instructions-vs-2-0) permits static Boolean `IF`/`CALLNZ`, `LOOP`, `REP` and subroutines, while the [`vs_2_x` table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-vs-instructions-vs-2-x) first adds `IFC`, `BREAK`/`BREAKC`/`BREAKP`, predicate `IF`/`CALLNZ`, `SETP` and instruction predication. MojoShader had neither a complete exact-`vs_2_0` opcode gate nor those operand/token version checks. Before repair, Software accepted all eight well-formed exact-2.0 probes (including pixel and vertex predicated `MOV`); EasyGL accepted seven and rejected predicate `CALLNZ` only accidentally in its later GLSL path. The shared parser now applies the complete `vs_2_0` allowlist, distinguishes Boolean from predicate operands and gates predicated instruction tokens at Shader Model 2.x or later without constraining `2_x`/software/3.0. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 123 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader Shader Model 2.x/3 profile validation and lifecycle/content closure remain SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-407 | Reject `LOOP` in pixel Shader Model 2.x | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after completing the `ps_2_x` versus `ps_3_0` instruction-table comparison.** Microsoft's [`ps_2_x` table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-ps-2-x) permits the count-only `REP`/`ENDREP` form but omits `LOOP`/`ENDLOOP`; the latter first appears in the [`ps_3_0` table](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-ps-3-0). MojoShader's stage-generic loop validator accepted a well-formed `ps_2_x` loop, and both Software and EasyGL consequently created the invalid Effect before repair. The focused pre-fix probes failed independently on both renderers; after repair the direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 124 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. The same complete table review confirmed that `TEXLDL`, the other relevant 3.0-only pixel opcode in MojoShader's table, was already rejected by its existing state validator. Broader Shader Model 3 profile/operand validation and lifecycle/content closure remain SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-408 | Enforce D3D9 matrix source and aliasing restrictions | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after following MojoShader's explicit matrix-validation FIXME into the Microsoft instruction contracts.** Microsoft's [`m4x3` pixel contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/m4x3---ps) states the rule shared by the matrix family: the matrix base `src1` may not be swizzled or negated, and `dst` may not be the same register as vector `src0`. MojoShader only validated destination masks and replicated the matrix rows. Both Software and EasyGL therefore accepted all three otherwise-well-formed `ps_2_0` discriminators: negated `src1`, swizzled `src1`, and an initialized `r0` used as both destination and vector source. The common replication helper now validates those operands once for `M4X4`, `M4X3`, `M3X4`, `M3X3` and `M3X2` in both shader stages before synthesizing the row sources. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 127 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass; existing exact matrix execution tests provide positive coverage for retained legal forms. MojoShader decoded both absolute forms without checking the program version, so Software and EasyGL each accepted all four otherwise-valid `vs_2_0`/`ps_2_0` discriminators. The managed parser patch retains negate everywhere but rejects both absolute forms below 3.0 in either stage. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 131 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass; existing Shader Model 3 programs remain positive controls. The separate Shader Model 3 all-or-none absolute constant-read rule and broader profile/lifecycle/content closure remain SOFTWARE-164/165 work; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-410 | Enforce consistent Shader Model 3 absolute float-constant reads | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after completing the source-modifier contract left explicit in MojoShader's parser FIXME.** Microsoft's [vertex](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-vs-registers-modifiers-source) and [pixel](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-modifiers-source) Shader Model 3 rules require every float-constant read in one shader to use `abs`/`-abs`, or none of them to use either absolute form. Before repair, both Software and EasyGL accepted all nine independent first-invalid probes: `ps_1_1 r2`, `ps_1_4 r6`, `ps_2_0 r12`, `ps_2_x r32`, `ps_3_0 r32`, `vs_1_1 r12`, `vs_2_0 r12`, `vs_2_x r32` and `vs_3_0 r32`. Broader register/operand and lifecycle/content closure remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |

| SOFTWARE-416 | Enforce fixed vertex-profile instruction-slot limits | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the next profile-boundary audit found that the shared parser counted instruction slots but never validated the result.** Microsoft's [vertex-profile comparison](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-vs-differences) fixes `vs_1_1` at 128 slots and both `vs_2_0` and `vs_2_x` at 256. | SOFTWARE-417 | Enforce the fixed `ps_2_0` arithmetic and texture instruction-slot budgets | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after separate category-boundary programs falsified both compiled paths.** Microsoft's [`ps_2_0` profile](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-2-0) fixes independent limits of 64 arithmetic and 32 texture slots. MojoShader accumulated only one undifferentiated diagnostic count and never enforced either budget, so Software and EasyGL accepted otherwise-valid programs containing 65 `MOV` slots and 33 `TEXLD` slots. The shared parser now maintains exact-profile arithmetic and texture counters, treats `TEXKILL`/`TEXLD` as the one texture slot specified by D3D9 rather than reusing internal aggregate costs, and rejects the first excess slot before backend emission. Paired 64/32 maximum programs remain accepted. Capability-dependent `ps_2_x` and `ps_3_0` maxima remain under SOFTWARE-164/165 instead of being guessed; the distinct `ps_1_x` category/phase limits were subsequently closed by SOFTWARE-418. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 183 then-current isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests passed; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-418 | Enforce pixel Shader Model 1.x arithmetic/texture budgets with phase and co-issue accounting | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after eight independent maximum-plus-one programs falsified both compiled paths.** Microsoft's [pixel-profile comparison](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-differences) fixes `ps_1_1..1_3` at four texture plus eight arithmetic slots and `ps_1_4` at six plus eight per phase. The shared parser enforced none of them. Its new category counters recognize the [published per-opcode costs](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-ps-1-x): zero-slot `NOP`, two-slot `DP4/CMP` in 1.2/1.3, dual-category `TEXBEML`, two-slot `BEM`, one-slot texture operations and RGB/alpha co-issued arithmetic pairs sharing one slot. A valid `PHASE` resets only the 1.4 per-phase counters. The suite proves ordinary 1.1 and 1.4 arithmetic/texture maxima, 1.2 double-slot arithmetic, `TEXBEML`, 32 extra NOPs, eight legal co-issued pairs, and eight arithmetic slots in each of two 1.4 phases; every corresponding first excess is rejected. SOFTWARE-458 later corrected the ps_1_4 texture-limit controls: their former `MOV`-then-`TEXKILL` order violated the independently measured texture-before-arithmetic rule, so the authentic fixtures now perform every texture operation before the final r0 write. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 192 then-current isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests passed. Remaining capability-dependent 2.x/3.0 slot ceilings and profile placement/operand rules stay under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-419 | Enforce pixel Shader Model 1.x destination-mask restrictions | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after three forged-bytecode probes exposed missing validation.** Software accepted every invalid program: a `ps_1_1 MOV` with an arbitrary `.rb` mask, `DP3` with an alpha-only mask and a legacy `TEXCOORD` with a partial mask. EasyGL accepted the first two and rejected the texture case only accidentally during later GLSL emission. Microsoft's [destination write-mask contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-modifiers-write-mask) restricts `ps_1_1..1_3` arithmetic destinations to `.rgba`, `.rgb` or `.a`, further restricts `DP3` to `.rgba`/`.rgb`, and forbids texture-address masks before the special `ps_1_4 TEXCRD`/`TEXLD` syntax. The common parser now enforces those rules before either backend emits code. Five controls retain all three ordinary 1.1 masks plus arbitrary 1.4 arithmetic and `TEXCRD` masks. SOFTWARE-458 later proved every Pixel Shader 1.x program must finish with all r0 components initialized; the RGB-only, alpha-only and arbitrary ps_1_4 mask controls now add complementary writes instead of relying on incomplete-output programs Microsoft rejects. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 196 then-current isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests passed. Remaining co-issue pairing and other placement/operand rules stay under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-420 | Reject ineligible pixel Shader Model 1.x co-issue opcodes | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after two opcode probes falsified both compiled paths.** Software and EasyGL accepted a co-issued legacy texture-address instruction even though Microsoft's [Shader Model 1.x overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-1-x) reserves pairing for arithmetic instructions, and accepted co-issued `DP4` despite Microsoft's [`DP4` contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dp4---ps) explicitly forbidding it because it occupies both RGB and alpha pipelines. In 1.4 the latter also evaded one arithmetic slot in SOFTWARE-418's otherwise-correct accounting. The common parser now rejects both classes before emission. RGB-then-alpha and alpha-then-RGB arithmetic pairs remain accepted; the reverse control matches the Windows-differential case documented in Wine's `d3d8/visual.c`. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 199 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Remaining structural pair adjacency/mask rules stay under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-421 | Enforce pixel Shader Model 1.x co-issue pairing structure | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after six additional executed probes plus a triple-pair guard exposed the absence of pair state.** After SOFTWARE-420's opcode gate, both renderers still accepted an orphaned co-issued instruction, RGB+RGB, full-mask+alpha, texture+alpha, `DP4`+alpha and a pair crossing `PHASE`; the old parser also had no state capable of rejecting a third co-issued instruction. Microsoft's [write-mask contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-modifiers-write-mask) defines co-issue as two instructions occupying the RGB and alpha pipelines, while its [1.x overview](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-1-x) limits pairing to arithmetic. The common context now retains the preceding instruction's opcode, destination mask and co-issue bit, requires exactly one eligible predecessor, rejects zero/two-pipeline opcodes and resets adjacency at `PHASE`; pre-1.4 pairs must use opposite RGB/alpha masks. It deliberately does not infer 1.4 pipeline identity solely from its arbitrary destination masks because `DP3` remains a vector-pipeline instruction even when writing alpha. Both valid pair orders survive. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 206 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Remaining 1.4 instruction-specific pipeline cases require measured evidence under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-422 | Enforce read-only shader input-register destinations | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after a register-access audit extended SOFTWARE-413's range-only proof.** Microsoft marks pixel color/interpolator banks and all vertex inputs read-only; Shader Model 2 pixel texture-coordinate registers are inputs too. Software accepted the ps_3_0 pixel case and both vertex cases; EasyGL rejected all six only through its later GLSL path. The common parser now rejects `REG_TYPE_INPUT` destinations in either stage and pixel Shader Model 2+ `REG_TYPE_TEXTURE` destinations, while preserving `DCL` destination tokens and legacy ps_1_x read/write texture registers. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 212 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Constant/control access, write-only output-source use and instruction-specific register permissions remain under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-423 | Enforce instruction-specific restricted shader destinations | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after thirteen forged `MOV` destinations exposed a broader register-access hole.** Software accepted ten illegal writes spanning float/integer/Boolean constant banks, loop state and predicate registers; EasyGL rejected all thirteen only in its later GLSL translation. Microsoft's profile register contracts allow float, integer and Boolean constant destinations only on `DEF`, `DEFI` and `DEFB`, allow predicate destinations only on `SETP`, and make sampler, miscellaneous and loop registers non-writable by ordinary instructions. The common parser now enforces those opcode-specific permissions before either backend emits code, while retaining descriptive `DCL` destinations and every legal special declaration/definition instruction. Software nevertheless accepted all six tested reads, including pixel `oDepth`; EasyGL accepted five and rejected only `oDepth` accidentally in its later GLSL translation. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 231 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. | SOFTWARE-425 | Enforce profile-specific vertex address-register access and conversion | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after nine address-register probes and a signed rounding oracle falsified both compiled paths.** Microsoft's address-register contract says `a0` cannot be read as an ordinary source, `vs_1_1` may write it only through `MOV`, `vs_2_0+` only through `MOVA`, and both conversions round float input to the nearest integer. Software accepted all six illegal source/destination programs and truncated legal `vs_1_1 MOV`; EasyGL accepted direct `vs_2_0`/`vs_3_0` reads, rejected the other illegal cases only downstream, and could not translate the legal `vs_1_1 MOV` float-to-integer assignment. The common parser now enforces the exact profile/opcode permissions, the GLSL `MOV` emitter and Software interpreter perform the documented signed round-to-nearest conversion, and three legal creation controls plus six rejection probes cover the access matrix. A direct Software program distinguishes `+1.6 -> +2` and `-1.6 -> -2` from truncation through relative constant reads. All 160 Software CTests and all 238 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Remaining source-operand and placement rules stay under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-426 | Reject direct sampler pseudo-register sources outside texture-load operand positions | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after three forged `MOV r#, s0` programs exposed inconsistent late rejection.** Microsoft's pixel and vertex sampler contracts explicitly say `s#` is a pseudo-register that cannot be read directly. The common parser tracks the current opcode and source ordinal, permits samplers only at the defined `TEXLD`/`TEXLDL` source-1 positions, and rejects every direct read before backend emission. Its original attempt to permit `TEXLDD` mistakenly named source 3; SOFTWARE-443 later corrected that instruction's sampler to its real source-1 position. Existing legal 2D/cube/volume pixel and vertex sampling proves the other allowed positions remain intact. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 241 then-current isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests passed. Typed flow-control and remaining source/placement rules stay under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-427 | Restrict integer and Boolean constant sources to their flow-control operands | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after four forged Shader Model 3 `MOV` programs falsified both compiled paths.** Microsoft documents `i#` as used only by `LOOP`/`REP` and `b#` as static flow-control state. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 245 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Predicate, label, loop-counter and miscellaneous source restrictions remain under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-428 | Restrict predicate, label and loop-counter sources to their control positions | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after six forged Shader Model 3 `MOV` programs and a Microsoft-contract differential falsified Software.** Software accepted ordinary pixel and vertex reads of `p0`, `l0` and `aL`; EasyGL rejected all six only during later GLSL translation. Common source-token validation now enforces those exact ordinary instruction positions, with a dedicated flag preserving the extra predicate token; the separately parsed relative-address token remains valid. Existing executed predication, conditional, break, subroutine, nested-loop and relative-address cases retain every legal path. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 251 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. SOFTWARE-429 subsequently closes relative-`aL` loop-scope legality; miscellaneous-input placement and remaining operand rules stay under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-429 | Enforce D3D9 relative-source register and loop-scope semantics | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after published Microsoft register contracts and a corroborating Wine D3DX differential overturned SOFTWARE-387's premise and falsified both compiled paths.** The documented D3D9 matrix, independently reproduced by Wine's builtin assembler, accepts pixel `v0[aL]`, vertex `v0[aL]` and vertex `c0[aL]` only inside a `LOOP` body or a subroutine called from that body; it rejects those operands outside inherited loop scope, rejects pixel `v0[a0.x]`, and rejects pixel relative constants in every scope. Software and EasyGL had accepted the invalid `c0[aL]`, both rejected legal relative pixel input, and neither enforced inherited loop scope. The managed parser now validates that register/profile/address matrix, carries the loop context through called labels and exposes fixed addressable input arrays to GLSL. The corrected shared draw uses legal pixel `v0[aL]` and deterministically selects the blue minified mip. A legal relative vertex-constant subroutine also exposed EasyGL emitting the vertex coordinate/depth epilogue after every `RET`, outside any GLSL function; that epilogue now belongs only to the closing mainline return. Four invalid and four valid creation probes plus the corrected draw pass remain green in the focused Software runtime; all 160 Software CTests and the complete 259-test EasyGL compiled-Effect family pass, the latter on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. Broader compiled-profile/lifecycle/content work remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-430 | Enforce `ps_3_0` `vPos` declaration masks and condition-only `vFace` use | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after four forged programs falsified Software and three falsified EasyGL's common parser.** Microsoft's [`ps_3_0` register contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-registers-ps-3-0) requires `vPos` to be declared with exactly `.x`, `.y` or `.xy` and restricts scalar `vFace` to comparison conditions; compiler-produced D3D9 bytecode also uses `vFace` as CMP's condition source. Both renderers accepted forbidden `.z`, `.xyz` and full `vPos` declarations. Software additionally accepted `MOV oC0, vFace`; EasyGL rejected that case only accidentally during GLSL type checking. The common parser now enforces the position masks and permits `vFace` only in CMP's condition or the operands of SETP/IFC/BREAKC. Four rejection probes and positive `.x`/`.y`/`.xy` plus CMP controls pass in Software and EasyGL; the existing rendered `vPos`/`vFace` scene remains the output oracle. All 160 Software CTests and the complete 267-test EasyGL compiled-Effect family pass, the latter on isolated Mesa/Xvfb. This audit also proved the local `D3DXAssembleShader` DLL is Wine builtin rather than Microsoft-native, so SOFTWARE-387/428/429 and the parity ledger now attribute that corroborating differential honestly and rest their authority on published Microsoft contracts. Broader compiled-profile/lifecycle/content work remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-431 | Enforce and execute Shader Model 3 packed semantic declarations | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after eight behavioral probes falsified the shared parser and EasyGL translator.** Microsoft's [`ps_3_0` semantic declaration contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dcl-usage---ps) and [`vs_3_0` output contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dcl-usage-outut-register---vs) allow one physical register to carry multiple distinct semantics only on disjoint component masks, forbid duplicate semantics and require full masks for position/point-size outputs; vertex inputs have no partial-mask form. Before repair, both paths accepted seven overlapping, duplicate or forbidden-mask programs. EasyGL also collapsed two legal declarations on one register to the last declaration and rendered black instead of `(64,128,191,255)`; Software's raw register vectors happened to preserve the legal value but still accepted all invalid declarations. The managed parser now retains one declaration record per semantic, rejects overlaps/duplicates/forbidden masks, and the GLSL profile packs/unpacks masked semantic varyings through one physical register while retaining desktop built-in linkage. The direct Software runtime and all 275 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader compiled-profile/lifecycle/content work remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-432 | Assemble separately stored vertex semantics into masked Shader Model 3 pixel inputs | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after a follow-up discriminator falsified Software immediately after SOFTWARE-431.** The first legal packing proof used one VS physical register for both semantic declarations, so Software's semantic values contained identical full vectors and hid that its pixel prologue replaced all four destination components for each declaration. A second valid pair places TEXCOORD0.xy in `o1` and COLOR0.zw in `o2`, gives every unused component a conflicting decoy, and packs them into `v0.xy`/`v0.zw`. EasyGL produced exact `(64,128,191,255)` while pre-fix Software failed the new probe. Software's token IR now retains each DCL write mask and its pixel input prologue copies only the owned components into the shared physical register. Both legal packing controls, the seven invalid declarations, the direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 276 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader compiled-profile/lifecycle/content work remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-433 | Execute explicit and implicit D3D9 pixel centroid interpolation under MSAA | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after Shader Model 2 and 3 boundary discriminators falsified both compiled paths.** Microsoft's [`ps_2_0+` modifier contract](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-modifiers-ps-2-0) requires explicit `_centroid` inputs to interpolate at a covered point when the pixel center lies outside the primitive and applies the same behavior automatically to `COLOR` semantics. At one partially covered 4x-MSAA pixel, ordinary TEXCOORD interpolation correctly selected red, but both renderers' implicit COLOR path extrapolated from the uncovered center and selected red instead of green; both also rejected the explicit modifier as unsupported. Software now retains the declaration qualifier and substitutes a covered sample's barycentrics only for centroid inputs, across ordinary/quad triangle and line/wireframe execution. The managed GLSL path emits separate centroid varyings and recompiles only the linked vertex-shader variant needed by the pixel declarations, avoiding permanent duplication of every varying. Six SM2/SM3 output controls, the direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 282 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader compiled-profile/lifecycle/content work remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |

Software already preserved unwritten components and selected the correct result lanes. MojoShader instead assigned `DST`'s full `vec4` or `CRS`'s `vec3` directly to an `.xz` `vec2` lvalue, so Mesa rejected both Effects before drawing. The managed GLSL emitter now applies the destination mask to each constructed result before assignment. Two shared full-quad output discriminators preserve Y/W and require the X/Z results. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 284 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Remaining compiled-profile/lifecycle/content work stays under SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-435 | Enforce compiled-shader structured flow-control grammar and nesting limits | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after twelve malformed or over-depth shaders falsified the shared compiled path.** Software accepted all eleven original invalid Shader Model 3 probes, while EasyGL accepted seven and rejected four only when the malformed GLSL reached Mesa. Microsoft's [vertex](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-vs-instructions-flow-control) and [pixel](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/direct3dhlsl/dx9-graphics-reference-asm-ps-instructions-flow-control) contracts cap Shader Model 3 static and dynamic conditional nesting at 24 and combined `LOOP`/`REP` nesting at four; exact `vs_2_0` permits one loop/repeat level. Static-flow counts were subsequently closed by SOFTWARE-436; call-depth and broader profile/lifecycle/content work remain SOFTWARE-164/165. `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |

| SOFTWARE-441 | Enforce immediate constant definition contracts | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the real Microsoft compiler falsified both compiled paths.** `d3dcompiler_47.dll` rejects duplicate pixel `DEF`, duplicate pixel/vertex `DEFI` and duplicate pixel/vertex `DEFB` definitions (`X5594..X5603`), while uniquely accepting a repeated vertex `DEF`. It also rejects `DEFI` tuples outside `x,y=[0,255]`, `z=[-128,127]`, `w=0` at the definition itself, even when the constant is never read. Software accepted all thirteen invalid probes. EasyGL accepted the eight invalid integer-range/reserved-field probes and rejected the valid repeated vertex `DEF` only when Mesa saw a duplicate GLSL declaration. The common parser now enforces every duplicate and integer-field rule, replaces the retained vertex-float definition on a legal redefinition, and the GLSL profile emits replaceable vertex constant macros so the final value remains observable even in generated helper functions. Five valid boundary/redefinition probes, thirteen invalid probes and a full-quad output discriminator pass in both paths. The direct Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests and all 356 isolated EasyGL compiled-Effect tests pass. Broader profile/lifecycle/content work remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-442 | Reject duplicate compiled-shader declarations | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the real Microsoft compiler falsified both compiled paths.** `d3dcompiler_47.dll` rejects a repeated pixel or vertex sampler declaration whether its dimension agrees or conflicts (`X5769`/`X5771`), a repeated Shader Model 2 pixel texture or color input register (`X5219`), a second Shader Model 2 vertex declaration of the same input register (`X5632`) and a second vertex register carrying the same semantic (`X5637`). Software and EasyGL accepted all nine invalid programs. The common parser now rejects every repeated sampler and every pre-Shader-Model-3 repeated physical input declaration, and additionally enforces unique pre-Shader-Model-3 vertex semantics. Its Shader Model 3 packed-semantic exception remains limited to the already-proven disjoint-mask forms from SOFTWARE-431. An 8x8 point-filtered texture has a red base and blue lower mips; its raw U/V are constant while W varies from 0.125 to 0.5 across a 4x4 quad. | SOFTWARE-456 | Enforce exact strict-profile `MOV` source-component initialization | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after the SOFTWARE-455 matrix exposed a second, independent under-validation hole.** Real `d3dcompiler_47.dll` accepts `mov r0.x, c0.x; mov r1, r0.xxxx` in both `ps_2_0` and `vs_1_1`, but rejects the otherwise identical Y-swizzled source because `r0.y` was never initialized. | SOFTWARE-457 | Correct and enforce Shader Model 1.4 `TEXCRD`/`TEXLD` projective operands | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after Microsoft assembly invalidated the evidence behind SOFTWARE-375/376/390.** `d3dcompiler_47.dll` establishes the exact rules: ordinary `TEXCRD` writes `.xyz`; its sole projective form is `TEXCRD r#.xy, t#_dw.xyw`; `TEXLD` writes all components, accepts `_dz` only as `r#_dz.xyz`, and accepts `_dw` only as `t#_dw.xyw`. The former fixtures used forbidden `TEXCRD _dz`, missing selectors, arbitrary selectors and a `.xy` ordinary destination. Before repair both CNA compiled paths accepted all seven forged operand violations plus the invalid ordinary destination mask. Managed MojoShader patch 82 now enforces register class, selector, modifier and destination mask, and also rejects DZ/DW on unrelated arithmetic instructions. Three corrected output-level fixtures prove canonical `TEXCRD _dw`, `TEXLD _dz` and `TEXLD _dw` projection including zero-divisor behavior; SOFTWARE-376's destination-stage/LOD fixture now carries `.xyw`, and SOFTWARE-377's phase fixture now uses `.xyz`. The display-free Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests, 17 focused EasyGL tests and the complete 441-test isolated EasyGL compiled family pass. Broader phase placement/component-lifetime validation remains SOFTWARE-164/165; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |

They also accepted incomplete r0 outputs in `ps_1_1` and `ps_1_4`. A tenth Microsoft-negative control covers texture-stage destination reuse. Managed MojoShader patch 83 now tracks each phase's texture/arithmetic blocks, stage use separately from actual texture value writes, prior-phase XY/XYZ availability, post-`TEXDEPTH` r5 consumption, the marker's alpha invalidation and all four final r0 components. Eight Microsoft-positive controls retain marker-free color/TEXKILL use, RGB carry, alpha reinitialization, two legal phase blocks, a cross-phase dependent read, `TEXKILL` followed by a read of its unchanged coordinate, and post-marker `TEXDEPTH`. SOFTWARE-418's ps_1_4 texture-budget fixtures now place texture operations before output arithmetic, while SOFTWARE-419's partial destination-mask controls complete the remaining r0 channels; neither task relies on assembler-invalid evidence. The display-free Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests, three focused EasyGL tests and the complete 444-test isolated EasyGL compiled family pass. BEM's distinct operand/component contract and other opcode-specific rules remain explicit SOFTWARE-164/165 work; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful. |
| SOFTWARE-459 | Enforce Shader Model 1.4 `BEM` operand and component-initialization rules | ✅ | **Closed 2026-09-12 after Microsoft differential assembly falsified both compiled paths and corrected SOFTWARE-385's positive fixture.** The real `d3dcompiler_47.dll` accepts only constant or temporary source 0, only temporary source 1, and requires each temporary component selected for the operation's XY inputs to have been initialized. Software and EasyGL accepted five independent invalid programs using a texture source, a constant second source or a temporary with only X initialized. Managed MojoShader patch 84 enforces those register classes and derives the exact required XY source components from each swizzle, using pre-instruction liveness when the destination aliases a source. Four controls retain replicated-X swizzles, destination saturation, temporary negate/`_bx2` modifiers and a constant/temporary pair. The repaired SOFTWARE-385 arithmetic fixture initializes r1/r2 and uses valid `BEM r0.xy,c1,r2` while preserving its exact expected output. A marker-free valid program assembles successfully, disproving the earlier suspicion that `BEM` requires an explicit `PHASE`. The display-free Software runtime, all 160 Software CTests, three focused isolated EasyGL tests and the complete 446-test EasyGL compiled family pass; `CompiledEffects=false` remains truthful while SOFTWARE-164/165 stay open. |

---

## Why this backend, in the owner's own words

> "Toto není backend pro běžné hraní, ale mohl by být překvapivě hodnotný... Pro kvalitu CNA by
> mohl mít větší hodnotu než šestý hardwarový backend."
> (This isn't a backend for regular gameplay, but could be surprisingly valuable... For CNA's
> quality, it could have more value than a sixth hardware backend.)

Stated use cases (verbatim, translated): headless CI; deterministic screenshot tests; testing
without a GPU; verifying basic XNA primitive operations; server environments; diagnosing
differences between backends; fallback when GPU initialization fails.

**How this differs fundamentally from the `HEADLESS` backend** (see `plan_headless.md`): Headless
proves "this code ran, with these arguments, this many times, and nothing leaked" but never
produces a real pixel — `ReadBackbuffer()` just reports the last `Clear()` color for every pixel.
Software is the opposite: it actually rasterizes real triangles into a real, CPU-owned RGBA8
framebuffer, so `GetBackBufferData()`/`ReadBackbuffer()` return genuinely correct pixels — this is
the entire point. It gives this project golden-image-style pixel tests (see
`docs/graphics-backend-feature-matrix.md`) that need **no GPU, no display server, and no driver**
at all, unlike the existing EasyGL/BGFX/Vulkan pixel tests which all need a real GPU context even
when run under Xvfb.

---

## Historical v1 design decisions (superseded where the parity campaign says so)

1. **Correctness and determinism over performance.** This is explicitly not a real-time gameplay
   backend — no SIMD, no multithreading, no tiling/binning in v1. Plain scalar loops are fine; a
   slow-but-obviously-correct rasterizer is more valuable here than a fast one that's subtly wrong,
   since the whole point is to be a trustworthy reference/diagnostic tool.
2. **Vertex format inference by stride, not a new common-interface change.** `IVertexBufferBackend`
   only ever receives raw bytes + a stride (`SetData(const void*, int vertex_count,
   std::size_t stride_in_bytes)`) — no `VertexDeclaration` is threaded through it. This project
   already has an established precedent for inferring vertex layout from stride alone (see the
   WebGPU backend's own stride-keyed pipeline selection: 16/20/24/32-byte strides map to
   `VertexPositionColor`/`VertexPositionTexture`/`VertexPositionColorTexture`/
   `VertexPositionNormalTexture` respectively). Software reuses that exact convention rather than
   inventing a new one or making a larger, riskier shared-interface change. v1 supports the 16/20/24
   strides (position+color, position+texture, position+color+texture); 32-byte
   (position+normal+texture) is deferred since lighting is out of scope for v1 anyway.
3. **The CPU framebuffer/depth buffer are the backend's real state, not a fiction.** A color buffer
   (RGBA8) and a depth buffer (float32) sized to the current render target (bound `RenderTarget2D`,
   or the backbuffer's `PresentationParameters` size when none is bound) back every operation for
   real. `Present()` is a no-op by default (matches the headless/server/CI use cases) — see
   `SOFTWARE-70` for an optional, later, opt-in "blit to a real window for visual inspection" mode,
   which is explicitly NOT required for this backend's stated value proposition.
4. **No window by default**, mirroring `HEADLESS`'s own `GraphicsDevice`/`Game::Run()` integration
   (`plan_headless.md` design decision 2): `#ifdef CNA_BACKEND_HEADLESS` guards in
   `GraphicsDevice.cpp`'s constructor and `createOrAttachWindow()` are extended to also cover
   `CNA_BACKEND_SOFTWARE` (`#if defined(CNA_BACKEND_HEADLESS) || defined(CNA_BACKEND_SOFTWARE)`) —
   a small, mechanical extension of an existing pattern, not a new one.
5. **SpriteBatch reuses the same triangle rasterizer as 3D draws.** A `SpriteBatch::Draw()` call is
   just a textured quad (2 triangles) with a transform matrix; feeding it through the same
   rasterizer core used for `DrawPrimitivesEx` gives this backend a real, pixel-correct 2D sprite
   path essentially for free — a capability `HEADLESS` never had (its `SpriteBatch` only records
   call arguments, never renders anything).
6. **`BasicEffect` subset only, no lighting/fog in v1.** `GpuDrawParams`' `vertexColorEnabled`,
   `textureEnabled`/`texture0`, and `diffuseColor` (material color/alpha) drive v1 pixel shading.
   `lightingEnabled`, `fogEnabled`, `dualTexture`, `envMapping`, `skinned` are all explicitly out of
   scope for v1 (matches the owner's own stated minimal first-version list — see "Boundaries").
7. **Only two blend modes in v1**: `Opaque` (direct overwrite, no blend) and `AlphaBlend`
   (`SrcAlpha`/`InvSrcAlpha`, the two most common real XNA/FNA `BlendState` presets). Other
   `ApplyBlendState(...)` factor/op combinations fall back to `AlphaBlend` behavior in v1 rather
   than attempting a fully general blend-equation interpreter — a real scope limitation, recorded
   honestly rather than silently claimed complete (see `SOFTWARE-42`).
8. **Custom `Effect` (GLSL/HLSL/WGSL source) cannot execute on the CPU**, same limitation
   `HEADLESS-16` already documents for that backend: accepts any effect source without compiling
   it, but only actually renders correctly for effects whose `FillGpuDrawParams()` output maps onto
   what v1's fixed pixel-shading path understands (see design decision 6). A custom effect with
   genuinely custom shader logic will not visually match its GPU-backend rendering under Software —
   documented as a known, permanent-for-v1 limitation, not a bug.

---

## Historical v1 execution order

1. Phase S1 (CMake integration + skeleton) unblocks everything else, exactly as `plan_headless.md`
   Phase N1 did for that backend.
2. Phase S2 (framebuffer/render targets) and Phase S3 (vertex/index storage + format inference) are
   independent of each other and can be done in either order, but both must land before Phase S4.
3. Phase S4 (rasterizer core: transform → clip → rasterize → depth test) is the heart of this
   backend — get a single flat-colored, non-textured, non-blended triangle rendering and
   pixel-verified correct (`SOFTWARE-60`'s first test) before adding Phase S5's pixel-shading
   features on top.
4. Phase S5 (blending/texture/vertex-color) builds directly on S4's per-pixel loop.
5. Phase S6 (effect integration, SpriteBatch reuse) is the actual point of this backend — like
   `plan_headless.md` Phase N6, it should be verified continuously against Phase S4/S5's already-
   proven core, not left to the end.
6. Phase S7 (tests) — per this project's own convention (`CLAUDE.md`), add test coverage in the
   same task that implements each capability, not bolted on afterward. `SOFTWARE-60`'s pixel tests
   in particular are this backend's actual reason to exist — do not skip them.
7. Phase S8 (docs) — write `docs/software-backend.md` as capabilities land, not all at the end.

For every task: build the affected target(s), run the relevant tests, and do not mark a task ✅
without both.

---

Current clarification (`SOFTWARE-154`): the renderer descriptor still requests neither video nor a window and the device owns no window, but shared `GraphicsAdapter` enumeration may independently pin process-global platform video when a display is available. The old `SDL_WasInit()==0` assertion was therefore not a renderer invariant and has been replaced by direct descriptor/window proof. |

## Phase S2 — Framebuffer and render targets

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SOFTWARE-10 | CPU color framebuffer (RGBA8, `std::vector<uint8_t>`) sized to the backbuffer (`PresentationParameters`); real `Clear(r,g,b,a)` fills every pixel | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 (`Software_Smoke` Check B): `Clear(Color(20,40,60,255))` followed by `GetBackBufferData()` returns exactly that color for every pixel in the queried region — not a fiction, a real per-pixel write-then-read round trip. |
| SOFTWARE-11 | CPU depth buffer (`std::vector<float>`), real `ClearDepth`/depth-inclusive `Clear*` variants | ✅ | `SoftwareFramebuffer::ClearDepthValue()` implemented and wired into `ClearDepth`/`ClearColorAndDepth`/`ClearDepthAndStencil`/`ClearColorDepthAndStencil`. Not yet exercised by a dedicated depth-readback test (no public API reads the depth buffer directly) — depth *correctness* will be verified properly once Phase S4's depth-test-driven rasterizer lands and can be proven via occlusion (a nearer triangle correctly occluding a farther one), a much stronger test than reading the raw buffer. |
| SOFTWARE-12 | `SoftwareRenderTargetBackend`: `CreateRenderTarget2D`/`SetRenderTarget2D` binds an alternate CPU color+depth buffer pair sized to that target, instead of the default backbuffer pair | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 (`Software_Smoke` Check C): binding an 8×8 `RenderTarget2D`, clearing it to a distinct color, then unbinding and reading the backbuffer confirms both framebuffers are genuinely independent — the render target's clear never touched the backbuffer's own pixels. Since 2026-08-01, `mipMap=true` also retains a generated RGBA8 chain after unbind; the shared GDI regression verifies its pixels, readback and minified sampling. |
| SOFTWARE-13 | `ReadBackbuffer()`/`GraphicsDevice::GetBackBufferData()`: real `memcpy` from the currently-bound CPU framebuffer — no faking, this is the backend's core value proposition | ✅ | Implemented as a real per-pixel copy (not literally `memcpy`, since out-of-bounds region requests are zero-filled rather than reading garbage) from `CurrentFramebuffer()`. Verified by the same `Software_Smoke` Checks B/C above. |
| SOFTWARE-14 | `Present()`: no-op by default, matching the headless/CI/server use cases | ✅ | Implemented as a genuine no-op (`{}`), matching design decision 3. |

## Phase S3 — Vertex/index storage and format inference

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SOFTWARE-20 | `SoftwareVertexBufferBackend`: stores raw bytes + stride (real storage, not discarded, mirroring `HeadlessVertexBufferBackend`'s own `ShadowData()` pattern) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 (`Software_Smoke` Check D): real `VertexBuffer`s at strides 16 (`VertexPositionColor`)/20 (`VertexPositionTexture`)/24 (`VertexPositionColorTexture`) all round-trip real vertex data through `SetData()` without throwing. `Data()`/`Stride()` accessors exist for Phase S4's rasterizer to read from directly. |
| SOFTWARE-21 | `SoftwareIndexBufferBackend`: 16- and 32-bit, real storage | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 (`Software_Smoke` Check E): both a 16-bit and a 32-bit `IndexBuffer` round-trip real index data without throwing. A genuine bit-width mismatch (`SetData16` on a buffer declared 32-bit or vice versa) throws `std::runtime_error`, mirroring `HeadlessIndexBufferBackend`'s own precedent. |
| SOFTWARE-22 | Stride-based vertex format inference (design decision 2): 16→`VertexPositionColor`, 20→`VertexPositionTexture`, 24→`VertexPositionColorTexture`. 32-byte (`VertexPositionNormalTexture`) explicitly deferred (needs lighting, out of scope for v1) | ✅ | **Closed** (dispatch logic landed with Phase S4's rasterizer, `BuildGenericClipVertex`/`BuildPositionColorClipVertex`, verified by `Software_Rasterizer`/`Software_Effects`). The 32-byte deferral was itself later lifted by `SOFTWARE-82` (Phase S9, 2026-07-13), which added real stride-32 (`VertexPositionNormalTexture`, `EnvironmentMapEffect`) and stride-52 (`VertexPositionNormalTextureSkinned`, `SkinnedEffect`) dispatch once a real (if lighting-free) use for the normal existed. `GLTF-387` completed the canonical table with 48/60-byte rigid PBR, 56-byte coloured skinning and 68/76-byte skinned PBR, all reached by the glTF native-boundary sweep. The 60/76 forms carry UV1; SOFTWARE consumes bit 0 of the shared PBR selector and slot zero's transform for its base-colour-only fallback. |

## Phase S4 — Rasterizer core

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SOFTWARE-30 | Per-vertex CPU transform: `World * View * Projection` (matching CNA's existing `Matrix` row/column convention) → clip space | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13: uses CNA's own `Matrix::operator*` (row-major, row-vector — `combined = world*view*projection`) and the existing `Vector4::Transform(Vector3, const Matrix&)` helper, reusing established codebase math rather than reimplementing the transform. `DrawColoredPrimitives`/`DrawIndexedColoredPrimitives` now genuinely rasterize (matching `VertexPositionColor`'s fixed layout — Position at offset 0, packed RGBA8 Color at offset 12, per that method's own documented "equivalent to BasicEffect with VertexColorEnabled=true" contract). |
| SOFTWARE-31 | Perspective divide + viewport transform → screen-space X/Y/Z | ✅ | `ndc = clip.xyz / clip.w`; `screenX=(ndcX*0.5+0.5)*viewportWidth`, `screenY=(1-(ndcY*0.5+0.5))*viewportHeight` (Y-flip: NDC is Y-up, the framebuffer is Y-down/top-left-origin). Depth used directly as `ndcZ` with no remapping, since CNA's projection matrices (like real XNA/FNA/D3D) already produce a 0..1 post-divide Z range, not OpenGL's -1..1. |
| SOFTWARE-32 | Triangle rasterization core: edge-function/barycentric fill, per-pixel depth test (`LessEqual`, matching `DepthStencilState::Default`) against the bound depth buffer, write-on-pass | ✅ | Standard edge-function rasterizer, bounding-box-limited, accepting either triangle winding (no backface culling in v1 — a real, intentional simplification, see Boundaries). Verified via the new `Software_Rasterizer` CTest (5/5): a solid-color triangle renders the exact color at its center pixel; **order-independent depth occlusion proven directly** (Checks C/D draw a near-red and far-blue triangle in both possible orders — red wins both times, proving the depth test is real, not an accidental last-write-wins artifact). |
| SOFTWARE-33 | Perspective-correct attribute interpolation (vertex color, UV) across the triangle | ✅ | Vertex colors are premultiplied by `1/clip.w` before rasterization, interpolated linearly in screen space, then divided by the interpolated `1/w` at the end (the standard technique). Verified via `Software_Rasterizer` Check B: a red/green/blue triangle's centroid pixel is the exact barycentric average of all three vertex colors. UV/texture interpolation follows the same mechanism once Phase S5 adds texture sampling. |
| SOFTWARE-34 | Basic near-plane handling: at minimum, cull triangles entirely behind the near plane; full polygon near-plane clipping (splitting a triangle into 1-2 new triangles) is a known v1 limitation, flagged here for a likely follow-up task once basic rendering is proven correct | ✅ | Implemented as a per-vertex `clip.W <= 1e-5f` check in `TransformPositionColorVertex()` — if any of a triangle's 3 vertices fails it, the whole triangle is culled rather than rasterized with garbage post-divide coordinates. Not yet exercised by a dedicated test with geometry deliberately crossing the near plane (all `Software_Rasterizer` test geometry stays safely in front of the camera) — a real, acknowledged gap, not silently claimed fully verified. |

## Phase S5 — Pixel shading

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SOFTWARE-40 | Vertex-color modulation (`vertexColorEnabled` path) | ✅ | `TransformGenericVertex()` reads real per-vertex color at stride 16/24 (position+color strides) and forces opaque white when `GpuDrawParams.vertexColorEnabled` is false — matches real XNA: `BasicEffect.VertexColorEnabled` itself defaults to `false`, so a plain `BasicEffect` ignores vertex colors unless a game opts in. Discovered directly while debugging `Software_Rasterizer`'s initial post-Phase-S6 regression (see `SOFTWARE-50`'s notes) — a real behavioral fact, not a rasterizer bug. |
| SOFTWARE-41 | Nearest-neighbor texture sampling (`textureEnabled`/`texture0`), reading the already-stored CPU-side pixel data every `ITextureBackend` implementation already keeps | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 (`Software_Effects` Check A): a full-screen quad textured with a 2x2 checker (`Texture2D::CreateFromPixels`) samples the correct texel near two opposite corners. `params.texture0` supports both `SoftwareTextureBackend` and `SoftwareRenderTargetBackend`; a completed mipmapped render target exposes its generated levels through the same sampling path. The same CPU SpriteBatch path also applies `ColorMatrixEffect` after texture/tint and before blending. Bilinear sampling remains a v2 stretch goal. |
| SOFTWARE-42 | Exact XNA blending for the shared shaded raster path: all `Blend` factors, independent colour/alpha `BlendFunction`, `BlendFactor`, and post-blend channel masks. | ✅ | `ApplyBlendState()` stores all six factor/function ordinals and `SetBlendFactor()` stores the dynamic constant; every shaded CPU fragment evaluates them per channel. The shared Release GDI 2D pixel regression covers Opaque, premultiplied AlphaBlend, NonPremultiplied, Additive, independent equations and BlendFactor. `Software_Effects` uses `NonPremultiplied` for its straight-alpha blend case. |
| SOFTWARE-43 | `diffuseColor`/alpha modulation from `GpuDrawParams` (BasicEffect's material color) | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 (`Software_Effects` Check B): `BasicEffect.DiffuseColor=(0.5,0,0)` tints a white texture to half-intensity red. Applied after texture sampling, matching the order real BasicEffect/XNA shaders apply material color. |

## Phase S6 — Effect integration

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SOFTWARE-50 | Wire `DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` to the Phase S4/S5 rasterizer using `GpuDrawParams`' `vertexColorEnabled`/`textureEnabled`/`texture0`/`diffuseColor`/`worldColMajor` fields; `lightingEnabled`/`fogEnabled`/`dualTexture`/`envMapping`/`skinned` explicitly out of scope for v1 | ✅ | Verified 2026-07-13 via `Software_Effects` (5/5) and re-verified `Software_Rasterizer` (5/5) still passing through the now-real `DrawPrimitivesEx`/`DrawIndexedPrimitivesEx` overrides (previously only reachable via `IGraphicsBackend`'s own default fallback to `DrawColoredPrimitives`). Stride validated (16/20/24 only at closure; later extended by SOFTWARE-82 and `GLTF-387`). The original `textureEnabled && texture0==nullptr` refusal was replaced by `GLTF-387`'s opaque-white absent-base-map identity for CNAEXT PbrEffect/SkinnedPbrEffect; SOFTWARE-302/303 later established that every classic stock effect instead samples null as opaque black. **Real bug caught by this exact wiring**: `Software_Rasterizer`'s existing checks briefly went 0/5 the moment this override landed, because `BasicEffect.VertexColorEnabled` defaults to `false` in real XNA/FNA (verified directly in `BasicEffect.hpp`) — the fallback path `DrawColoredPrimitives` had been implicitly always treating color as enabled, masking this. Fixed by updating the test to explicitly set `VertexColorEnabled = true` (matching how a real game would), not by changing the (correct) new behavior. |
| SOFTWARE-51 | `SpriteBatch` reuses the same rasterizer for its quad draws (design decision 5) — a real, pixel-correct CPU `SpriteBatch` path | ✅ | `SoftwareSpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` builds its quad corners using the exact same formula as `EasyGLGraphicsBackend::EasyGLSpriteBatchBackend::Draw()` (destination/source rectangle, origin, rotation, `SpriteEffects` flip), then feeds two triangles directly into `RasterizeTriangleShaded()` in screen-pixel space (no `World*View*Projection` — SpriteBatch never uses one). `transformMatrix_` is applied as a 2D point transform on the already-placed corners. Verified via `Software_Effects` Check E: a solid-color texture drawn via `SpriteBatch::Draw()` lands at the exact requested screen position. Rotation/origin/`SpriteEffects` flip are implemented (reusing the proven formula) but not yet covered by a dedicated test — a real, acknowledged gap. |
| SOFTWARE-52 | Custom `ShaderEffect` (arbitrary GLSL/HLSL/WGSL source): accept without compiling (mirrors `HEADLESS-16`), document that only effects whose `FillGpuDrawParams()` output matches v1's fixed pixel-shading path will render correctly (design decision 8) | ✅ | Already implemented since Phase S1 (`SoftwareEffectBackend::CompileProgram()`): accepts any non-empty vertex/fragment source without compiling, mirroring `HEADLESS-16`. Not yet exercised by a dedicated Software test (Headless has one via `Headless_ResourceBackends`); the underlying behavior is identical and low-risk, so this is a documentation/test-coverage gap, not an implementation gap. |

## Phase S7 — Tests

| # | Task | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| SOFTWARE-60 | Deterministic pixel tests via real `GetBackBufferData()` readback, no GPU/display needed: clear-color test, single flat-colored triangle test, single textured quad test | ✅ | Done, and then some, across `Software_Smoke`/`Software_Rasterizer`/`Software_Effects` (16 checks total): clear-color (`Software_Smoke` B), flat-colored triangle + depth occlusion (`Software_Rasterizer`), textured quad + blending + `SpriteBatch` (`Software_Effects`). Did not need a `PixelTestGame`-style shared harness — each test file is its own small `Game` subclass reading pixels directly via `GetBackBufferData()`, mirroring the exact pattern established for `HEADLESS`'s own test suite. |
| SOFTWARE-61 | (Stretch goal) Cross-backend diagnostic test: render an identical simple scene on `SOFTWARE` and a real GPU backend, compare within a tolerance — directly serves the owner's stated "diagnostika rozdílů mezi backendy" use case, but depends on a real GPU backend being available in the test environment; may not be runnable in every CI | ✅ | **Closed 2026-07-13 via `SOFTWARE-84`** (Phase S9) — see that row for the implementation (a shared scene source built per-backend + a standalone comparator, run manually since `CNA_GRAPHICS_BACKEND` is a compile-time choice). |
| SOFTWARE-62 | CTest registration, mirroring `HEADLESS`'s own `cna_headless_test`-style CMake macro | ✅ | `cna_software_test()` CMake macro (mirrors `cna_headless_test()` exactly), 3 tests registered: `Software_Smoke`, `Software_Rasterizer`, `Software_Effects`, all labeled `"Software"`, no `SDL_VIDEODRIVER`/`DISPLAY` set. |
